```
1	# Sync State Machine and Per-Lock Error Isolation
2	
3	**Date**: 2026-04-20
4	**Issue**: [#1061](https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/issues/1061)
5	
6	## Problem
7	
8	When a single lock is temporarily unreachable during a slot sync, the entire slot gets disabled across all locks in the profile. This happens because:
9	
10	1. **Error misclassification**: Z-Wave transport errors (`FailedZWaveCommand`) from `get_usercode_from_node()` are not caught by the provider and fall through to the generic `except Exception` handler in the sync manager, which treats them as unexpected bugs.
11	2. **Profile-wide blast radius**: Both the circuit breaker and the generic exception handler call `_disable_slot`, which turns off the profile-level enabled switch, affecting all locks.
12	3. **Flag-based state is error-prone**: The sync manager uses three independent boolean/optional flags (`_dirty`, `_in_sync`, `_tick_in_sync`) that can theoretically enter contradictory states.
13	
14	## Solution Overview
15	
16	1. Replace the flag-based sync state with an explicit state machine enum.
17	2. Introduce a lock-level suspend mechanism that isolates failures to the affected lock.
18	3. Fix error classification in the Z-Wave provider.
19	4. Expose a `sync_status` attribute on the in-sync binary sensor for dashboard differentiation.
20	
21	## State Machine
22	
23	### States
24	
25	| State | Meaning | `is_on` | `sync_status` |
26	|---|---|---|---|
27	| `LOADING` | Waiting for valid entity states after startup | `None` | N/A |
28	| `SYNCED` | Lock code matches desired state | `True` | `synced` |
29	| `OUT_OF_SYNC` | Mismatch detected, queued for next tick | `False` | `out_of_sync` |
30	| `SYNCING` | Sync operation executing within current tick | `False` | `syncing` |
31	| `SUSPENDED` | Lock suspended, retries paused until lock recovers | `False` | `suspended` |
32	
33	### Transitions
34	
35	```
36	              +----------+
37	              | LOADING  |-------------+
38	              +----+-----+             |
39	                   |                   |
40	                   v                   v
41	            +----------+         +-------------+
42	            |  SYNCED   |<------>| OUT_OF_SYNC |<--+
43	            +----------+        +------+------+   |
44	                   ^                   |          |
45	                   |             +-----+----+     |
46	                   +-------------|  SYNCING  |----+
47	                                 +-----+-----+
48	                                       |
49	                                       v
50	                                 +-----------+
51	                                 | SUSPENDED |
52	                                 +-----------+
53	```
54	
55	| Edge | Trigger |
56	|---|---|
57	| LOADING -> SYNCED | Initial state resolves, already in sync |
58	| LOADING -> OUT_OF_SYNC | Initial state resolves, mismatch detected |
59	| SYNCED -> OUT_OF_SYNC | `_request_sync_check`: PIN changed, slot toggled, coordinator update |
60	| OUT_OF_SYNC -> SYNCING | Tick begins sync operation |
61	| SYNCING -> SYNCED | Sync operation succeeded |
62	| SYNCING -> OUT_OF_SYNC | Transient failure (`LockDisconnected`, `LockOperationFailed`), retry next tick |
63	| SYNCING -> SUSPENDED | Circuit breaker tripped or unexpected exception; sets lock-level suspend flag |
64	| SUSPENDED -> OUT_OF_SYNC | Lock-level suspend flag cleared (coordinator received successful update) |
65	
66	### Replacing existing flags
67	
68	| Old | New |
69	|---|---|
70	| `_dirty = True` | State is `OUT_OF_SYNC` (tick will act on it) |
71	| `_dirty = False` | State is `SYNCED`, `LOADING`, or `SUSPENDED` (tick skips) |
72	| `_in_sync = True` | State is `SYNCED` |
73	| `_in_sync = False` | State is `OUT_OF_SYNC`, `SYNCING`, or `SUSPENDED` |
74	| `_in_sync = None` | State is `LOADING` |
75	| `_tick_in_sync` | Eliminated; circuit breaker resets on explicit state transitions |
76	
77	### Tick behavior by state
78	
79	- **LOADING**: Resolve slot state. If valid, transition to SYNCED or OUT_OF_SYNC.
80	- **SYNCED**: No work. Skip.
81	- **OUT_OF_SYNC**: Check lock-level suspend flag. If suspended, transition to SUSPENDED. Otherwise, check circuit breaker, transition to SYNCING, perform sync operation, transition to result state.
82	- **SYNCING**: N/A (transient state, only exists during tick execution).
83	- **SUSPENDED**: No work. Skip. Resume happens via lock-level flag being cleared, which triggers `_request_sync_check` through the coordinator listener.
84	
85	### `_request_sync_check` (replaces `_mark_dirty`)
86	
87	Called on coordinator updates and entity state changes. Behavior by current state:
88	
89	| Current state | Action |
90	|---|---|
91	| SYNCED | Transition to OUT_OF_SYNC if `calculate_in_sync` returns False |
92	| OUT_OF_SYNC | No-op (already queued) |
93	| SYNCING | No-op (sync in progress) |
94	| SUSPENDED | No-op (waiting for lock-level flag to clear) |
95	| LOADING | No-op (initial load handles it) |
96	
97	## Lock-Level Suspend Flag
98	
99	### Location
100	
101	A `suspended` boolean on the `LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator`. The coordinator already owns the reconnection lifecycle (`_reset_backoff`, connection transition handling), so the flag sits next to the recovery logic that clears it.
102	
103	### Setting the flag
104	
105	Any `SlotSyncManager` that hits a circuit breaker trip or a generic exception sets `self._coordinator.suspended = True`. This suspends all sync managers for that lock.
106	
107	### Clearing the flag
108	
109	Cleared inside the coordinator when `_async_update_data` succeeds (same code path where `_reset_backoff` runs). Clearing the flag triggers the coordinator listener, which calls `_request_sync_check` on all sync managers. Since the flag is now cleared, managers in SUSPENDED state can transition to OUT_OF_SYNC.
110	
111	### Effect on sync managers
112	
113	In the tick, before transitioning OUT_OF_SYNC -> SYNCING, the sync manager checks `self._coordinator.suspended`. If True, it transitions to SUSPENDED instead of attempting sync. This prevents other slots from wasting attempts on a lock that's already proven unreachable.
114	
115	## Error Handling Taxonomy
116	
117	### Exception-to-transition mapping
118	
119	| Exception | Fault | Transition | Side effect |
120	|---|---|---|---|
121	| `CodeRejectedError` / `DuplicateCodeError` | The code | SYNCING -> (disable slot) -> SYNCED | `_disable_slot` (profile-wide), `slot_disabled` repair issue |
122	| `LockDisconnected` / `LockOperationFailed` | The lock | SYNCING -> OUT_OF_SYNC | None (retry next tick) |
123	| `Exception` (generic) | Unknown | SYNCING -> SUSPENDED | Sets lock-level suspend flag, `slot_suspended` repair issue |
124	| Circuit breaker tripped | The lock | OUT_OF_SYNC -> SUSPENDED | Sets lock-level suspend flag, `slot_suspended` repair issue |
125	
126	### Principle
127	
128	Only errors where the code/PIN is provably wrong should disable the slot profile-wide. Everything else is a lock-level problem that should be isolated.
129	
130	## Repair Issues
131	
132	| Trigger | Issue key | Scope | Cleared when |
133	|---|---|---|---|
134	| `CodeRejectedError` / `DuplicateCodeError` | `slot_disabled_{entry_id}_{slot_num}` | Profile-wide | User re-enables slot |
135	| Circuit breaker / generic exception | `slot_suspended_{entry_id}_{lock_entity_id}` | Per-lock | Lock recovers and all slots reach SYNCED |
136	
137	The `slot_suspended` issue description lists all affected slots on that lock.
138	
139	## Z-Wave Provider Fix
140	
141	### Problem
142	
143	`get_usercode_from_node()` calls at `zwave_js.py:448` and `489` can raise exceptions from the `zwave_js_server` library (e.g., `FailedZWaveCommand` for Z-Wave errors 202, 204). These are not subclasses of `LockDisconnected` or `LockOperationFailed`, so they fall through to the generic `except Exception` handler.
144	
145	### Fix
146	
147	Wrap the `get_usercode_from_node()` calls in try/except blocks that catch exceptions from the library and re-raise as `LockDisconnected`. The wrapping is scoped to these specific call sites (post-operation verification polls), not a blanket catch of all `FailedZWaveCommand` errors. This ensures future unhandled call sites surface through the generic handler as suspensions rather than being silently misclassified.
148	
149	## In-Sync Binary Sensor Attribute
150	
151	### New attribute: `sync_status`
152	
153	Exposed as an extra state attribute on `InSyncBinarySensorEntity`, derived from the sync manager's current state.
154	
155	| State | `sync_status` value |
156	|---|---|
157	| LOADING | Not exposed (entity unavailable or `None`) |
158	| SYNCED | `synced` |
159	| OUT_OF_SYNC | `out_of_sync` |
160	| SYNCING | `syncing` |
161	| SUSPENDED | `suspended` |
162	
163	This allows dashboards to style differently based on the sync status (e.g., different colors or icons for "actively syncing" vs "suspended, needs attention").
164	
165	## Files Changed
166	
167	1. **`sync.py`**: State machine enum. `_request_sync_check` replaces `_mark_dirty`. Circuit breaker and generic exception transition to SUSPENDED and set lock-level flag. Per-lock `slot_suspended` repair issue. Remove `_disable_slot` calls from circuit breaker and generic exception paths.
168	2. **`coordinator.py`**: `suspended` flag on `LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator`. Cleared on successful data update.
169	3. **`binary_sensor.py`**: `InSyncBinarySensorEntity` exposes `sync_status` extra state attribute.
170	4. **`zwave_js.py`**: Wrap `get_usercode_from_node()` calls to catch exceptions and re-raise as `LockDisconnected`.
171	5. **`strings.json` / `translations/en.json`**: New `slot_suspended` translation key for per-lock repair issue.
172	6. **`repairs.py`**: Handle `slot_suspended` issue fixability.
173	
```

1	"""
2	Slot sync manager — owns desired vs actual reconciliation.
3	
4	Compares entity states (active, PIN, name) against coordinator data (actual lock
5	code) and drives set/clear operations to reconcile. Uses a state machine
6	(SyncState enum) with periodic tick for retries and per-lock suspension on
7	repeated failures.
8	
9	Tick interval: 2 seconds (TICK_INTERVAL)
10	Circuit breaker: 3 attempts within 5 minutes (MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS, SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW)
11	"""
12	
13	from __future__ import annotations
14	
15	from collections.abc import Callable
16	from dataclasses import dataclass
17	from datetime import datetime
18	import logging
19	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
20	
21	from homeassistant.components.binary_sensor import DOMAIN as BINARY_SENSOR_DOMAIN
22	from homeassistant.components.sensor import DOMAIN as SENSOR_DOMAIN
23	from homeassistant.components.text import DOMAIN as TEXT_DOMAIN
24	from homeassistant.const import (
25	    CONF_NAME,
26	    CONF_PIN,
27	    STATE_OFF,
28	    STATE_ON,
29	    STATE_UNAVAILABLE,
30	    STATE_UNKNOWN,
31	)
32	from homeassistant.core import Event, EventStateChangedData, HomeAssistant, callback
33	from homeassistant.helpers import entity_registry as er
34	from homeassistant.helpers.event import (
35	    TrackStates,
36	    async_track_state_change_event,
37	    async_track_state_change_filtered,
38	    async_track_time_interval,
39	)
40	from homeassistant.helpers.issue_registry import (
41	    IssueSeverity,
42	    async_create_issue,
43	    async_delete_issue,
44	)
45	from homeassistant.util import dt as dt_util
46	
47	from .const import (
48	    ATTR_ACTIVE,
49	    ATTR_CODE,
50	    DOMAIN,
51	    MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS,
52	    SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW,
53	    TICK_INTERVAL,
54	)
55	from .exceptions import CodeRejectedError, LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed
56	from .models import SlotCode, SyncState
57	from .util import async_disable_slot
58	
59	if TYPE_CHECKING:
60	    from .coordinator import LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator
61	    from .models import LockCodeManagerConfigEntry
62	    from .providers import BaseLock
63	
64	
65	_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
66	
67	
68	@dataclass(frozen=True)
69	class SlotState:
70	    """
71	    Snapshot of entity states for a slot on a specific lock.
72	
73	    Used by SlotSyncManager to compare desired (entity) vs actual
74	    (coordinator) state. Includes raw HA state strings (rather than
75	    parsed values) because sync logic needs to distinguish between
76	    "off" and "unavailable" — both look like the same parsed bool but
77	    mean different things for retry decisions.
78	    """
79	
80	    active_state: str
81	    pin_state: str
82	    name_state: str | None
83	    code_state: str
84	    coordinator_code: str | SlotCode | None
85	
86	
87	class SlotSyncManager:
88	    """
89	    Manage sync state for a single lock x slot combination.
90	
91	    Compares desired state (from entity states: active, PIN) against actual
92	    state (from coordinator data) and drives set/clear operations to reconcile.
93	
94	    Uses a state machine (SyncState) with five states: LOADING, IN_SYNC,
95	    OUT_OF_SYNC, SYNCING, SUSPENDED. State changes mark the slot for
96	    re-evaluation; reconciliation happens on the next tick. Includes circuit
97	    breaker protection that suspends the lock after repeated sync failures.
98	
99	    The in-sync binary sensor entity reads manager.in_sync and
100	    manager.sync_status for display.
101	
102	    State mutation rules:
103	        - ``_request_sync_check`` transitions IN_SYNC -> OUT_OF_SYNC or
104	          SUSPENDED -> OUT_OF_SYNC for immediate UI feedback.
105	        - ``_async_tick_impl`` is the single authoritative place for all
106	          other state transitions, circuit breaker, sync operations,
107	          and ``_last_set_pin`` changes.
108	
109	    Lifecycle methods (async_start, async_stop) are idempotent and re-entrant.
110	    """
111	
112	    def __init__(
113	        self,
114	        hass: HomeAssistant,
115	        ent_reg: er.EntityRegistry,
116	        config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry,
117	        coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,
118	        lock: BaseLock,
119	        slot_num: int,
120	        state_writer: Callable[[bool | None], None],
121	    ) -> None:
122	        """Initialize the sync manager."""
123	        self._hass = hass
124	        self._ent_reg = ent_reg
125	        self._config_entry = config_entry
126	        self._coordinator = coordinator
127	        self._lock = lock
128	        self._slot_num = int(slot_num)
129	        self._state_writer = state_writer
130	
131	        self._log_prefix = (
132	            f"{config_entry.entry_id} ({config_entry.title}): "
133	            f"{lock.lock.entity_id} slot {slot_num}"
134	        )
135	
136	        # Unique ID components for entity discovery
137	        entry_id = config_entry.entry_id
138	        lock_entity_id = lock.lock.entity_id
139	        base_uid = f"{entry_id}|{slot_num}"
140	        self._unique_ids: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {
141	            CONF_PIN: (TEXT_DOMAIN, f"{base_uid}|{CONF_PIN}"),
142	            CONF_NAME: (TEXT_DOMAIN, f"{base_uid}|{CONF_NAME}"),
143	            ATTR_ACTIVE: (BINARY_SENSOR_DOMAIN, f"{base_uid}|{ATTR_ACTIVE}"),
144	            ATTR_CODE: (SENSOR_DOMAIN, f"{base_uid}|{ATTR_CODE}|{lock_entity_id}"),
145	        }
146	
147	        # Sync state machine — single source of truth replacing _dirty,
148	        # _in_sync, and _tick_in_sync.
149	        self._state: SyncState = SyncState.LOADING
150	        self._entity_id_map: dict[str, str] = {}
151	        self._tracked_entity_ids: set[str] = set()
152	
153	        # Track the last PIN we successfully set, so we can detect when the
154	        # configured PIN changes while the lock code is UNKNOWN (masked/write-only).
155	        # This is in-memory only — on restart, _last_set_pin is None, which means
156	        # UNKNOWN slots will be treated as out-of-sync and re-set. This is the
157	        # safest behavior: it guarantees the lock has the correct PIN even if the
158	        # config changed while HA was down, at the cost of one extra set per
159	        # masked/write-only slot on every restart.
160	        self._last_set_pin: str | None = None
161	
162	        # Circuit breaker
163	        self._sync_attempt_count: int = 0
164	        self._sync_attempt_first: datetime | None = None
165	
166	        # Invalid state tracking (for initial load)
167	        self._logged_invalid_state: bool = False
168	
169	        # State tracking
170	        self._state_tracking_unsub: Callable[[], None] | None = None
171	        self._coordinator_unsub: Callable[[], None] | None = None
172	        self._tick_unsub: Callable[[], None] | None = None
173	        self._tracking_all_states: bool = False
174	        self._started = False
175	
176	    @property
177	    def in_sync(self) -> bool | None:
178	        """Return current sync state (None = not yet determined)."""
179	        if self._state is SyncState.LOADING:
180	            return None
181	        return self._state is SyncState.IN_SYNC
182	
183	    @property
184	    def sync_status(self) -> str | None:
185	        """Return granular sync status for dashboard display."""
186	        if self._state is SyncState.LOADING:
187	            return None
188	        return self._state.value
189	
190	    async def async_start(self) -> None:
191	        """Start the sync manager -- discover entities, subscribe, initial tick."""
192	        if self._started:
193	            return
194	        self._started = True
195	        self._setup_state_tracking()
196	        self._setup_coordinator_listener()
197	        self._tick_unsub = async_track_time_interval(
198	            self._hass, self._async_tick, TICK_INTERVAL
199	        )
200	        await self._async_tick()
201	
202	    def async_stop(self) -> None:
203	        """Stop the sync manager -- unsubscribe tick and listeners. Idempotent."""
204	        if not self._started:
205	            return
206	        self._started = False
207	        if self._tick_unsub:
208	            self._tick_unsub()
209	            self._tick_unsub = None
210	        self._cleanup_state_tracking()
211	        if self._coordinator_unsub:
212	            self._coordinator_unsub()
213	            self._coordinator_unsub = None
214	        self._reset_sync_tracker()
215	
216	    # -- State resolution ----------------------------------------------------
217	
218	    def _get_entity_state(self, key: str) -> str | None:
219	        """Get entity state by role key."""
220	        entity_id = self._entity_id_map.get(key)
221	        if not entity_id:
222	            return None
223	        state = self._hass.states.get(entity_id)
224	        return state.state if state else None
225	
226	    def _build_entity_id_map(self) -> bool:
227	        """Build and cache entity IDs for this slot from the entity registry."""
228	        missing = False
229	        for key, (domain, unique_id) in self._unique_ids.items():
230	            if key in self._entity_id_map:
231	                continue
232	            ent_id = self._ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(domain, DOMAIN, unique_id)
233	            if not ent_id:
234	                missing = True
235	                continue
236	            self._entity_id_map[key] = ent_id
237	        self._tracked_entity_ids = set(self._entity_id_map.values())
238	        return not missing
239	
240	    def _ensure_entities_ready(self) -> bool:
241	        """
242	        Ensure all dependent entities exist with valid states.
243	
244	        The name entity (CONF_NAME) is optional — STATE_UNKNOWN is its
245	        normal state when no name is configured.
246	
247	        When the slot is inactive (active entity is OFF), PIN and code
248	        sensor entities are allowed to be STATE_UNKNOWN since the sync
249	        manager only needs to know the slot is off to proceed (clear or
250	        confirm already cleared). This prevents disabled slots from
251	        being stuck in LOADING forever.
252	        """
253	        # Collect all states in a single pass
254	        states: dict[str, str | None] = {}
255	        for key in (CONF_PIN, CONF_NAME, ATTR_ACTIVE, ATTR_CODE):
256	            if key not in self._entity_id_map:
257	                return False
258	            state = self._get_entity_state(key)
259	            if state is None or state == STATE_UNAVAILABLE:
260	                _LOGGER.debug("%s: Waiting for %s state", self._log_prefix, key)
261	                return False
262	            states[key] = state
263	
264	        # Active entity must always have a definite state
265	        if states[ATTR_ACTIVE] == STATE_UNKNOWN:
266	            _LOGGER.debug("%s: Waiting for %s state", self._log_prefix, ATTR_ACTIVE)
267	            return False
268	
269	        # Name is always optional (STATE_UNKNOWN = no name configured)
270	        # PIN and code sensor can be unknown when the slot is inactive
271	        slot_inactive = states[ATTR_ACTIVE] == STATE_OFF
272	        for key in (CONF_PIN, ATTR_CODE):
273	            if states[key] == STATE_UNKNOWN and not slot_inactive:
274	                _LOGGER.debug("%s: Waiting for %s state", self._log_prefix, key)
275	                return False
276	
277	        return True
278	
279	    def _resolve_slot_state(self) -> SlotState | None:
280	        """
281	        Resolve slot state from current entity and coordinator data.
282	
283	        All state reads are sync (hass.states.get) with no awaits between them,
284	        ensuring atomicity on Home Assistant's single-threaded event loop.
285	        """
286	        if not self._build_entity_id_map():
287	            return None
288	
289	        if self._slot_num not in self._coordinator.data:
290	            _LOGGER.debug(
291	                "%s: Slot not in coordinator data, skipping",
292	                self._log_prefix,
293	            )
294	            return None
295	
296	        if not self._ensure_entities_ready():
297	            return None
298	
299	        active_state = self._get_entity_state(ATTR_ACTIVE)
300	        pin_state = self._get_entity_state(CONF_PIN)
301	        name_state = self._get_entity_state(CONF_NAME)
302	        code_state = self._get_entity_state(ATTR_CODE)
303	
304	        if active_state is None or pin_state is None or code_state is None:
305	            return None
306	
307	        coordinator_code = self._coordinator.data.get(self._slot_num)
308	        return SlotState(
309	            active_state=active_state,
310	            pin_state=pin_state,
311	            name_state=name_state,
312	            code_state=code_state,
313	            coordinator_code=coordinator_code,
314	        )
315	
316	    # -- Sync calculation ----------------------------------------------------
317	
318	    def calculate_in_sync(self, slot_state: SlotState) -> bool:
319	        """
320	        Calculate whether slot should be in sync.
321	
322	        Active (state=ON): PIN should match code on lock.
323	        Inactive (state=OFF): Code on lock should be empty.
324	
325	        For UNKNOWN codes (masked/write-only): in sync only if the configured
326	        PIN matches what we last successfully set. This ensures that PIN
327	        changes trigger a re-set even when the lock code is unreadable, and
328	        that taking over a slot with an existing masked code triggers a set.
329	        """
330	        lock_code = (
331	            slot_state.coordinator_code
332	            if slot_state.coordinator_code is not None
333	            else slot_state.code_state
334	        )
335	        if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
336	            if lock_code is SlotCode.UNREADABLE_CODE:
337	                return slot_state.pin_state == self._last_set_pin
338	            if lock_code is SlotCode.EMPTY:
339	                # If we recently set a PIN on this slot and it matches the
340	                # configured PIN, trust the set — the provider may not have
341	                # caught up yet (eventual consistency, e.g. Schlage cloud API).
342	                return (
343	                    self._last_set_pin is not None
344	                    and slot_state.pin_state == self._last_set_pin
345	                )
346	            return slot_state.pin_state == lock_code
347	        # active_state == STATE_OFF: slot should be cleared
348	        # The "" check covers the fallback path where coordinator_code is None
349	        # and lock_code comes from the code sensor entity state (which returns ""
350	        # for SlotCode.EMPTY).
351	        return lock_code is SlotCode.EMPTY or lock_code == ""
352	
353	    # -- Sync execution ------------------------------------------------------
354	
355	    async def _perform_sync(self, slot_state: SlotState) -> None:
356	        """
357	        Execute sync operation (set or clear usercode).
358	
359	        Raises CodeRejectedError, LockDisconnected, or propagates any exception.
360	        Error handling is done by the caller (_async_tick).
361	        """
362	        if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
363	            await self._lock.async_internal_set_usercode(
364	                self._slot_num,
365	                slot_state.pin_state,
366	                slot_state.name_state,
367	                source="sync",
368	            )
369	            self._last_set_pin = slot_state.pin_state
370	            # Track set operations toward circuit breaker. Clear operations
371	            # don't increment the counter (expected to always succeed).
372	            self._record_sync_attempt()
373	            _LOGGER.debug("%s: Set usercode", self._log_prefix)
374	        else:
375	            await self._lock.async_internal_clear_usercode(
376	                self._slot_num, source="sync"
377	            )
378	            self._last_set_pin = None
379	            _LOGGER.debug("%s: Cleared usercode", self._log_prefix)
380	
381	    async def _disable_slot(self, reason: str) -> None:
382	        """Disable the slot and create a repair issue."""
383	        try:
384	            await async_disable_slot(
385	                self._hass,
386	                self._ent_reg,
387	                self._config_entry.entry_id,
388	                self._slot_num,
389	                reason=reason,
390	                lock_name=self._lock.display_name,
391	                lock_entity_id=self._lock.lock.entity_id,
392	            )
393	        except Exception:
394	            _LOGGER.exception(
395	                "%s: Failed to disable slot via service call",
396	                self._log_prefix,
397	            )
398	            # Fallback: create repair issue directly so the user is notified
399	            # even though the switch service call failed
400	            async_create_issue(
401	                self._hass,
402	                DOMAIN,
403	                f"slot_disabled_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._slot_num}",
404	                is_fixable=True,
405	                is_persistent=True,
406	                severity=IssueSeverity.WARNING,
407	                translation_key="slot_disabled",
408	                translation_placeholders={
409	                    "slot_num": str(self._slot_num),
410	                    "reason": reason,
411	                },
412	            )
413	        finally:
414	            self._reset_sync_tracker()
415	
416	    def _suspend_lock(self, reason: str) -> None:
417	        """Suspend this lock and create a per-lock repair issue."""
418	        self._state = SyncState.SUSPENDED
419	        self._coordinator.suspend_slot_sync_mgrs()
420	        self._reset_sync_tracker()
421	        self._write_state()
422	
423	        issue_id = (
424	            f"slot_suspended_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}"
425	        )
426	        async_create_issue(
427	            self._hass,
428	            DOMAIN,
429	            issue_id,
430	            is_fixable=True,
431	            is_persistent=True,
432	            severity=IssueSeverity.WARNING,
433	            translation_key="slot_suspended",
434	            translation_placeholders={
435	                "lock_entity_id": self._lock.lock.entity_id,
436	                "lock_name": self._lock.display_name or self._lock.lock.entity_id,
437	                "reason": reason,
438	            },
439	        )
440	
441	    # -- Attempt tracking + circuit breaker ----------------------------------
442	
443	    def _reset_sync_tracker(self) -> None:
444	        """Reset the sync attempt tracker."""
445	        self._sync_attempt_count = 0
446	        self._sync_attempt_first = None
447	
448	    def _record_sync_attempt(self) -> None:
449	        """
450	        Record a sync attempt toward circuit breaker counter.
451	
452	        Called for set operations. Successful clear operations and transient
453	        LockDisconnected errors are not tracked since they represent
454	        transient lock communication issues, not persistent failures.
455	        """
456	        now = dt_util.utcnow()
457	        if (
458	            self._sync_attempt_first is not None
459	            and now - self._sync_attempt_first > SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW
460	        ):
461	            self._sync_attempt_count = 0
462	            self._sync_attempt_first = None
463	
464	        if self._sync_attempt_first is None:
465	            self._sync_attempt_first = now
466	        self._sync_attempt_count += 1
467	
468	    def _sync_attempts_exceeded(self) -> bool:
469	        """Check if sync attempts exceeded the limit within the time window."""
470	        if self._sync_attempt_count < MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS:
471	            return False
472	        if self._sync_attempt_first is None:
473	            return False
474	        return dt_util.utcnow() - self._sync_attempt_first <= SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW
475	
476	    # -- Orchestration -------------------------------------------------------
477	
478	    def _write_state(self) -> None:
479	        """Notify the entity to write Home Assistant state."""
480	        self._state_writer(self.in_sync)
481	
482	    @callback
483	    def _request_sync_check(self, *_args: Any) -> None:
484	        """
485	        Request a sync check on the next tick.
486	
487	        Transitions IN_SYNC -> OUT_OF_SYNC if calculate_in_sync returns False
488	        (also resets circuit breaker since the sync target changed).
489	        Transitions SUSPENDED -> OUT_OF_SYNC if the coordinator is no longer suspended.
490	        No-op for LOADING, OUT_OF_SYNC, SYNCING.
491	        """
492	        if self._state is SyncState.IN_SYNC:
493	            slot_state = self._resolve_slot_state()
494	            if slot_state is not None and not self.calculate_in_sync(slot_state):
495	                self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
496	                self._reset_sync_tracker()
497	                self._write_state()
498	        elif self._state is SyncState.SUSPENDED:
499	            if not self._coordinator.slot_sync_mgrs_suspended:
500	                self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
501	                self._write_state()
502	
503	    @callback
504	    def _request_sync_check_if_relevant(
505	        self, event: Event[EventStateChangedData]
506	    ) -> None:
507	        """
508	        Request sync check only if the state change is for a tracked entity.
509	
510	        Used by the catch-all state tracking fallback to avoid unnecessary
511	        checks on every HA state change. Falls back to always-checking if
512	        entity IDs haven't been discovered yet.
513	        """
514	        if not self._tracked_entity_ids or (
515	            event.data["entity_id"] in self._tracked_entity_ids
516	        ):
517	            self._request_sync_check()
518	
519	    async def _async_tick(self, _now: datetime | None = None) -> None:
520	        """Periodic reconciliation tick."""
521	        if not self._started:
522	            return
523	
524	        # Try upgrading before the state check — catch-all mode may prevent
525	        # _request_sync_check from firing for entities not yet tracked
526	        self._try_upgrade_state_tracking()
527	
528	        if self._state in (SyncState.IN_SYNC, SyncState.SYNCING, SyncState.SUSPENDED):
529	            return
530	
531	        await self._async_tick_impl()
532	
533	    async def _async_tick_impl(self) -> None:
534	        """
535	        Core tick logic — called from _async_tick for LOADING and OUT_OF_SYNC states.
536	
537	        This is the single authoritative place for all sync state mutations:
538	        circuit breaker tracking, sync operations, and ``_last_set_pin``
539	        changes.
540	        """
541	        slot_state = self._resolve_slot_state()
542	        if slot_state is None:
543	            # State resolution failed — stay in current state and retry.
544	            return
545	
546	        expected_in_sync = self.calculate_in_sync(slot_state)
547	
548	        # -- LOADING: detect initial sync state without performing operations --
549	        if self._state is SyncState.LOADING:
550	            if slot_state.active_state not in (STATE_ON, STATE_OFF):
551	                if not self._logged_invalid_state:
552	                    _LOGGER.debug(
553	                        "%s: Active entity has invalid state '%s', waiting "
554	                        "for valid state (ON/OFF)",
555	                        self._log_prefix,
556	                        slot_state.active_state,
557	                    )
558	                    self._logged_invalid_state = True
559	                return
560	
561	            if expected_in_sync:
562	                self._state = SyncState.IN_SYNC
563	                if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
564	                    async_delete_issue(
565	                        self._hass,
566	                        DOMAIN,
567	                        f"slot_disabled_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._slot_num}",
568	                    )
569	                # Per-lock issue: clear regardless of slot active state
570	                async_delete_issue(
571	                    self._hass,
572	                    DOMAIN,
573	                    f"slot_suspended_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}",
574	                )
575	            else:
576	                self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
577	
578	            _LOGGER.debug(
579	                "%s: Initial state loaded, state=%s",
580	                self._log_prefix,
581	                self._state.value,
582	            )
583	            self._write_state()
584	            return
585	
586	        # -- OUT_OF_SYNC: check coordinator suspend flag, then attempt sync --
587	        if self._coordinator.slot_sync_mgrs_suspended:
588	            self._state = SyncState.SUSPENDED
589	            self._write_state()
590	            return
591	
592	        if expected_in_sync:
593	            # Became in sync without us doing anything (external change)
594	            self._state = SyncState.IN_SYNC
595	            self._reset_sync_tracker()
596	            self._write_state()
597	            if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
598	                async_delete_issue(
599	                    self._hass,
600	                    DOMAIN,
601	                    f"slot_disabled_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._slot_num}",
602	                )
603	            # Per-lock issue: clear regardless of slot active state
604	            async_delete_issue(
605	                self._hass,
606	                DOMAIN,
607	                f"slot_suspended_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}",
608	            )
609	            return
610	
611	        # Circuit breaker check (set operations only)
612	        if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON and self._sync_attempts_exceeded():
613	            _LOGGER.error(
614	                "%s: Sync attempts exceeded (%s in %s window), suspending lock",
615	                self._log_prefix,
616	                self._sync_attempt_count,
617	                SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW,
618	            )
619	            self._suspend_lock(
620	                f"Lock **{self._lock.lock.entity_id}**: slot "
621	                f"**{self._slot_num}** failed to sync after "
622	                f"{self._sync_attempt_count} consecutive attempts. "
623	                f"The lock may be rejecting the code silently or "
624	                f"experiencing communication issues. "
625	                f"Sync has been suspended for this lock. It will "
626	                f"resume automatically when the lock recovers.",
627	            )
628	            return
629	
630	        # Perform sync
631	        self._state = SyncState.SYNCING
632	        self._write_state()
633	        try:
634	            await self._perform_sync(slot_state)
635	        except CodeRejectedError as err:
636	            _LOGGER.error("%s: Code rejected: %s", self._log_prefix, err)
637	            await self._disable_slot(
638	                f"Lock **{err.lock_entity_id}**: slot **{err.code_slot}** "
639	                f"has been disabled. {err}\n\n"
640	                f"Fix the issue and re-enable the slot.",
641	            )
642	            # After disable, the slot active switch turns off. The next
643	            # _request_sync_check will see the slot as in-sync (no code
644	            # desired, no code on lock). Set to OUT_OF_SYNC so the next
645	            # tick resolves to IN_SYNC.
646	            self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
647	            return
648	        except (LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed) as err:
649	            _LOGGER.info(
650	                "%s: Lock operation failed during %s usercode: %s. Will retry on next tick.",
651	                self._log_prefix,
652	                "set" if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON else "clear",
653	                err,
654	            )
655	            self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
656	            return
657	        except Exception as err:
658	            _LOGGER.exception(
659	                "%s: Unexpected error during %s usercode. "
660	                "Sync suspended for this lock to prevent infinite retry loop. "
661	                "Error: %s: %s",
662	                self._log_prefix,
663	                "set" if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON else "clear",
664	                type(err).__name__,
665	                err,
666	            )
667	            self._suspend_lock(
668	                f"Lock **{self._lock.lock.entity_id}**: slot **{self._slot_num}** "
669	                f"encountered an unexpected error during sync. This may indicate a bug "
670	                f"in the lock code manager integration. Check logs for details and "
671	                f"report this issue.\n\nError: {type(err).__name__}: {err}",
672	            )
673	            return
674	        else:
675	            # Sync succeeded — refresh coordinator to verify.
676	            # Skip for push providers — they update coordinator optimistically
677	            # via push_update() and refreshing from cache could read stale data.
678	            if not self._lock.supports_push:
679	                try:
680	                    await self._coordinator.async_refresh()
681	                except Exception:
682	                    _LOGGER.exception(
683	                        "%s: Coordinator refresh failed after sync operation. "
684	                        "Sync may have succeeded but verification failed. "
685	                        "Will retry on next tick.",
686	                        self._log_prefix,
687	                    )
688	                    self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
689	                    return
690	
691	        # Check if sync actually worked
692	        slot_state = self._resolve_slot_state()
693	        if slot_state is not None and self.calculate_in_sync(slot_state):
694	            self._state = SyncState.IN_SYNC
695	            self._reset_sync_tracker()
696	            self._write_state()
697	            if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
698	                async_delete_issue(
699	                    self._hass,
700	                    DOMAIN,
701	                    f"slot_disabled_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._slot_num}",
702	                )
703	            # Per-lock issue: clear regardless of slot active state
704	            async_delete_issue(
705	                self._hass,
706	                DOMAIN,
707	                f"slot_suspended_{self._config_entry.entry_id}_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}",
708	            )
709	        else:
710	            self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
711	            self._write_state()
712	
713	    # -- State tracking subscriptions ----------------------------------------
714	
715	    def _setup_coordinator_listener(self) -> None:
716	        """Subscribe to coordinator updates."""
717	        self._coordinator_unsub = self._coordinator.async_add_listener(
718	            self._request_sync_check
719	        )
720	
721	    @callback
722	    def _cleanup_state_tracking(self) -> None:
723	        """Clean up state tracking subscription if one exists."""
724	        if self._state_tracking_unsub:
725	            self._state_tracking_unsub()
726	            self._state_tracking_unsub = None
727	            self._tracking_all_states = False
728	
729	    @callback
730	    def _try_upgrade_state_tracking(self) -> None:
731	        """
732	        Upgrade catch-all state tracking to targeted if entities are now available.
733	
734	        Called at the start of each tick (outside any callback), so it is safe
735	        to modify subscriptions here without timing issues.
736	        """
737	        if not self._tracking_all_states or not self._build_entity_id_map():
738	            return
739	
740	        assert self._state_tracking_unsub is not None
741	        self._state_tracking_unsub()
742	        self._state_tracking_unsub = async_track_state_change_event(
743	            self._hass,
744	            self._tracked_entity_ids,
745	            self._request_sync_check,
746	        )
747	        self._tracking_all_states = False
748	        _LOGGER.debug(
749	            "%s: Upgraded from catch-all to targeted state tracking",
750	            self._log_prefix,
751	        )
752	
753	    @callback
754	    def _setup_state_tracking(self) -> None:
755	        """
756	        Set up state change tracking for dependent entities.
757	
758	        If all entity IDs are available, tracks only those specific entities.
759	        Otherwise, tracks all state changes via a catch-all subscription that
760	        filters by tracked entity IDs in _request_sync_check_if_relevant. The catch-all
761	        is upgraded to targeted tracking in _try_upgrade_state_tracking(), which
762	        runs at the start of each tick (safe because it's outside a callback).
763	        """
764	        self._cleanup_state_tracking()
765	
766	        if self._build_entity_id_map():
767	            self._state_tracking_unsub = async_track_state_change_event(
768	                self._hass,
769	                self._tracked_entity_ids,
770	                self._request_sync_check,
771	            )
772	            self._tracking_all_states = False
773	        else:
774	            tracker = async_track_state_change_filtered(
775	                self._hass,
776	                TrackStates(True, set(), set()),
777	                self._request_sync_check_if_relevant,
778	            )
779	            self._state_tracking_unsub = tracker.async_remove
780	            self._tracking_all_states = True
781	            _LOGGER.debug(
782	                "%s: Waiting for dependent entities, tracking all state changes",
783	                self._log_prefix,
784	            )
785

41	class LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator(DataUpdateCoordinator[dict[int, str | SlotCode]]):
42	    """Class to manage usercode updates."""
43	
44	    def __init__(self, hass: HomeAssistant, lock: BaseLock, config_entry: Any) -> None:
45	        """Initialize the usercode update coordinator."""
46	        self._lock = lock
47	        self._drift_unsub: Callable[[], None] | None = None
48	        self._connection_unsub: Callable[[], None] | None = None
49	        # Disable periodic polling when push updates are supported.
50	        # Polling is still used for initial load.
51	        update_interval = None if lock.supports_push else lock.usercode_scan_interval
52	        super().__init__(
53	            hass,
54	            _LOGGER,
55	            name=f"{DOMAIN} {lock.lock.entity_id}",
56	            update_method=self.async_get_usercodes,
57	            update_interval=update_interval,
58	            config_entry=config_entry,
59	        )
60	        self.data: dict[int, str | SlotCode] = {}
61	        self._slot_sync_mgrs_suspended: bool = False
62	        self._config_entry = config_entry
63	        self._consecutive_failures: int = 0
64	        self._original_update_interval: timedelta | None = update_interval
65	
66	        # Set up drift detection timer for locks with hard_refresh_interval
67	        if lock.hard_refresh_interval:
68	            self._drift_unsub = async_track_time_interval(
69	                hass,
70	                self._async_drift_check,
71	                lock.hard_refresh_interval,
72	                cancel_on_shutdown=True,
73	            )
74	
75	        if lock.connection_check_interval:
76	            # Periodic connection checks drive reconnect handling for non-push providers.
77	            self._connection_unsub = async_track_time_interval(
78	                hass,
79	                self._async_connection_check,
80	                lock.connection_check_interval,
81	                cancel_on_shutdown=True,
82	            )
83	
84	    @property
85	    def lock(self) -> BaseLock:
86	        """Return the lock."""
87	        return self._lock
88	
89	    def get_expected_pin(self, slot_num: int) -> str | None:
90	        """Return configured PIN for a slot, or None if disabled/unconfigured."""
91	        slot_data = get_entry_config(self._config_entry).slot(slot_num)
92	        if not slot_data.get(CONF_ENABLED):
93	            return None
94	        return slot_data.get(CONF_PIN) or None
95	
96	    def slot_expects_pin(self, slot_num: int) -> bool:
97	        """Return whether LCM expects a PIN on this slot (enabled with PIN)."""
98	        return self.get_expected_pin(slot_num) is not None
99	
100	    @staticmethod
101	    def _normalize_keys(
102	        data: dict[Any, str | SlotCode],
103	    ) -> dict[int, str | SlotCode]:
104	        """Coerce slot keys to ``int``. Raises ValueError/TypeError if a key cannot be cast."""
105	        return {int(k): v for k, v in data.items()}
106	
107	    @callback
108	    def push_update(self, updates: dict[int, str | SlotCode]) -> None:
109	        """Push one or more slot updates and notify listening entities."""
110	        if not updates:
111	            return
112	
113	        new_data = {**self.data, **self._normalize_keys(updates)}
114	        # Skip update if data hasn't actually changed to avoid redundant logging
115	        # and unnecessary listener notifications
116	        if new_data == self.data:
117	            return
118	
119	        # A successful push update proves the lock is reachable, so reset
120	        # backoff to re-enable drift checks and normal polling.
121	        self._reset_backoff()
122	
123	        self.async_set_updated_data(new_data)
124	
125	    def _apply_backoff(self) -> None:
126	        """Increment failure counter and apply exponential backoff if threshold met."""
127	        self._consecutive_failures += 1
128	        if self._consecutive_failures >= BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD:
129	            backoff_secs = min(
130	                BACKOFF_INITIAL_SECONDS
131	                * 2 ** (self._consecutive_failures - BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD),
132	                BACKOFF_MAX_SECONDS,
133	            )
134	            if self._original_update_interval is not None:
135	                new_interval = timedelta(seconds=backoff_secs)
136	                if new_interval != self.update_interval:  # type: ignore[has-type]
137	                    self.update_interval = new_interval
138	                    _LOGGER.warning(
139	                        "Update failed %d consecutive times for %s, "
140	                        "backing off polling interval to %ds",
141	                        self._consecutive_failures,
142	                        self._lock.lock.entity_id,
143	                        backoff_secs,
144	                    )
145	            else:
146	                _LOGGER.warning(
147	                    "Update failed %d consecutive times for %s, "
148	                    "suppressing drift checks until recovery",
149	                    self._consecutive_failures,
150	                    self._lock.lock.entity_id,
151	                )
152	
153	        if self._consecutive_failures == POLL_FAILURE_ALERT_THRESHOLD:
154	            async_create_issue(
155	                self.hass,
156	                DOMAIN,
157	                f"lock_offline_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}",
158	                is_fixable=False,
159	                is_persistent=True,
160	                severity=IssueSeverity.WARNING,
161	                translation_key="lock_offline",
162	                translation_placeholders={
163	                    "lock_entity_id": self._lock.lock.entity_id,
164	                },
165	            )
166	
167	    @property
168	    def slot_sync_mgrs_suspended(self) -> bool:
169	        """Return whether slot sync operations are suspended for this lock."""
170	        return self._slot_sync_mgrs_suspended
171	
172	    def suspend_slot_sync_mgrs(self) -> None:
173	        """
174	        Suspend slot sync managers for this lock.
175	
176	        Called by any SlotSyncManager that hits a circuit breaker or
177	        unexpected error. All sync managers for this lock will see the
178	        flag and stop retrying. Cleared automatically on recovery via
179	        _reset_backoff (successful poll or push update).
180	        """
181	        self._slot_sync_mgrs_suspended = True
182	        _LOGGER.info("Slot sync suspended for %s", self._lock.lock.entity_id)
183	        # Notify listeners so other SlotSyncManagers for this lock
184	        # transition to SUSPENDED on their next _request_sync_check.
185	        self.async_update_listeners()
186	
187	    def _reset_backoff(self) -> None:
188	        """Reset failure counter and restore original update interval."""
189	        self._slot_sync_mgrs_suspended = False
190	        if self._consecutive_failures > 0:
191	            _LOGGER.info(
192	                "Lock %s recovered after %d consecutive failures",
193	                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
194	                self._consecutive_failures,
195	            )
196	            self._consecutive_failures = 0
197	            if self._original_update_interval is not None:
198	                self.update_interval = self._original_update_interval
199	        # Unconditionally clear lock_offline issue on any successful poll.
200	        # Runs outside the if-block so it also clears persisted issues that
201	        # survive HA restarts (where _consecutive_failures resets to 0).
202	        async_delete_issue(
203	            self.hass,
204	            DOMAIN,
205	            f"lock_offline_{self._lock.lock.entity_id}",
206	        )
207	
208	    async def async_get_usercodes(self) -> dict[int, str | SlotCode]:
209	        """Fetch usercodes from the provider, normalize slot keys to int, and apply backoff handling."""
210	        try:
211	            data = await self._lock.async_internal_get_usercodes()
212	        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
213	            self._apply_backoff()
214	            # During cold start (before the first successful poll), do not
215	            # raise UpdateFailed. That would fail the initial refresh and
216	            # keep coordinator-backed entities unavailable until a
217	            # successful poll completes.
218	            if not self.last_update_success:
219	                return {}
220	            raise UpdateFailed from err
221	
222	        self._reset_backoff()
223	        return self._normalize_keys(data)
224	
225	    async def _async_drift_check(self, now: datetime) -> None:
226	        """
227	        Perform periodic drift detection.
228	
229	        Hard refresh re-fetches codes from the lock to detect out-of-band changes
230	        (e.g., codes changed at the lock's keypad). If changes are detected,
231	        updates coordinator data and notifies listeners.
232	        """
233	        # Skip if we haven't successfully loaded initial data yet
234	        if not self.last_update_success:
235	            return
236	
237	        if self._consecutive_failures >= BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD:
238	            _LOGGER.debug(
239	                "Skipping drift check for %s (in backoff after %d failures)",
240	                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
241	                self._consecutive_failures,
242	            )
243	            return
244	
245	        _LOGGER.debug(
246	            "Performing drift detection hard refresh for %s",
247	            self._lock.lock.entity_id,
248	        )
249	        try:
250	            new_data = self._normalize_keys(
251	                await self._lock.async_internal_hard_refresh_codes()
252	            )
253	        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
254	            self._apply_backoff()
255	            _LOGGER.warning(
256	                "Drift detection hard refresh failed for %s: %s",
257	                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
258	                err,
259	            )
260	            return
261	
262	        # Push subscription retry is handled by the config entry state
263	        # listener and connection transition handler — no need to retry here.
264	
265	        if new_data != self.data:
266	            _LOGGER.debug(
267	                "Drift detected for %s, updating coordinator data",
268	                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
269	            )
270	            self.async_set_updated_data(new_data)
271	
272	    async def _async_connection_check(self, now: datetime) -> None:
273	        """Poll connection state so providers can resubscribe on reconnect."""
274	        try:
275	            await self._lock.async_internal_is_integration_connected()
276	        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
277	            _LOGGER.debug(
278	                "Connection check failed for %s: %s", self._lock.lock.entity_id, err
279	            )
280	
281	    async def async_shutdown(self) -> None:
282	        """Shut down the coordinator and clean up resources."""
283	        if self._drift_unsub:
284	            self._drift_unsub()
285	            self._drift_unsub = None
286	        if self._connection_unsub:
287	            self._connection_unsub()
288	            self._connection_unsub = None
289	        await super().async_shutdown()

> AGENT

Here's what the current resilience machinery actually consists of. There are **seven** distinct mechanisms across three files:

| # | Mechanism | Location | State it owns |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Operation rate limit** (serialize + 2s spacing per lock) | `_base.py:185` `_execute_rate_limited` | `_aio_lock`, `_last_operation_time`, `_min_operation_delay` |
| 2 | **Poll backoff** (exp backoff 60s→1800s after 3 fails) | `coordinator.py:125` `_apply_backoff`/`_reset_backoff` | `_consecutive_failures`, `update_interval` |
| 3 | **Sync circuit breaker** (3 sets / 5min → suspend) | `sync.py:441` `_record_sync_attempt`/`_sync_attempts_exceeded` | `_sync_attempt_count`, `_sync_attempt_first` |
| 4 | **Sync tick + cross-tick retry** (2s tick re-attempts OUT_OF_SYNC) | `sync.py:519` `_async_tick` | `_state` (SyncState enum) |
| 5 | **Per-lock suspend flag** (gate that pauses all sync mgrs) | `coordinator.py:172` + `sync.py:416` | `_slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` |
| 6 | **Drift-check timer** (skips while in backoff) | `coordinator.py:225` | reuses #2's counter |
| 7 | **Connection-check timer** (reconnect detection) | `coordinator.py:272` | timer only |

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The core complexity is one concept fragmented into three.** "This lock is failing" is tracked by *three* independent pieces of state — `_consecutive_failures` (poll layer), `_sync_attempt_count` (sync layer), and `_slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` (the gate). Recovery is bridged by hand: `_reset_backoff` happens to clear *both* the counter and the suspend flag.

**They're also not coupled where they should be.** A disconnected lock backs off polling to 30 min (#2), but the 2s sync tick (#4) keeps hammering `_execute_rate_limited`, which fails fast at its connection check every 2s. The suspend flag (#5) only gets set by the circuit breaker (#3) or an unexpected exception — never by plain disconnection — so nothing tells the sync tick "this lock is down, stop trying."

**The two genuinely separable concerns** are *operation pacing* (#1, self-contained and fine) versus *lock-health / failure tracking* (#2+#3+#5, the tangle). The tick (#4) is really the retry engine, and the timers (#6, #7) are scheduling.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

So yes — this can collapse meaningfully, most likely into a single per-lock "circuit/health" primitive that both the coordinator and the sync managers read, plus the existing operation-pacing helper kept as-is.

Before I propose concrete approaches, one scoping question that shapes everything:

> TOOL

> AGENT

Good — that unlocks the real consolidation. One more design fork, because it changes the shape of the unified primitive.

I found the three failure signals aren't *quite* identical in meaning. Two of them are genuinely different failure semantics:

- **"Lock unreachable"** (connectivity) — poll fails or a set raises `LockDisconnected`. Affects the whole lock; recovers when the lock comes back.
- **"Code won't take"** (slot-specific) — here's the subtle one: the sync circuit breaker (#3) increments *only after a set call returns successfully* (`sync.py:369`), i.e. "I set it, the call succeeded, but the slot still never reaches in-sync after 3 tries." That's silent rejection / eventual-consistency-never-converging for *one specific PIN on one slot* — not the lock being down.

Today both are funneled into the same `_slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` lock-level flag, which is part of why it's tangled.

> AGENT

Now the approaches. The two scopes share the same *bookkeeping* (count failures, remember when they started, decide "may I attempt now?") but differ in their *action on trip* (lock = slow down polling; slot = suspend + repair issue) and their *recovery trigger* (lock = next success; slot = slot reaches in-sync). So the shared piece should be the accounting; the policy stays with each owner.

Three ways to shape that shared piece:

**A. A pure `CircuitBreaker` value type (accounting only) — recommended.**
A small dependency-free class (no `hass`, no I/O): holds `failure_count` + `first_failure_at`, and exposes `record_failure()`, `record_success()`, `is_open`, and `next_attempt_at`. The exponential-backoff math (now in `coordinator.py:128-133`) and the N-in-window logic (now in `sync.py:456-474`) both become this one type, configured per instance: lock-level uses consecutive-failure backoff; slot-level uses trip-after-3-in-5-min. The coordinator derives its poll interval from `next_attempt_at`; the sync tick reads `is_open` instead of the suspend flag. Owners keep their own trip *actions* (repair issues, interval changes).
- *Pros:* deletes the most duplicated state; trivially unit-testable in isolation (the current logic is buried in async HA callbacks and hard to test directly); both call sites read one consistent answer. *Cons:* one new type to learn (~60 lines).

**B. A formal `LockHealth` state machine (CLOSED / OPEN / HALF_OPEN with probe).**
Same idea but with explicit half-open probing on recovery.
- *Pros:* textbook circuit breaker. *Cons:* recovery here is already driven by an external success signal (a poll or push succeeds), so HALF_OPEN probing is machinery we'd never exercise — YAGNI.

**C. Minimal shared helpers (two functions over a tiny struct).**
Extract `record_result()` / `should_attempt()` operating on a shared dataclass, leave state living where it is.
- *Pros:* smallest diff. *Cons:* doesn't actually consolidate ownership — the state stays scattered across coordinator and sync manager, so the "three signals" problem only half-goes-away.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The reason A is the real win here isn't line count — it's **testability and single-source-of-truth**. Right now the backoff math lives inside `async_get_usercodes`/`_apply_backoff` (reachable only by failing real polls) and the window logic lives inside a 2s async tick. Neither can be tested without driving the whole HA event loop. A pure value type with no `hass` reference turns "does our backoff cap at 1800s?" and "does 3-in-5-min trip?" into plain synchronous unit tests — which matters given your memory notes a standing goal to strengthen the provider/sync test suite.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

My recommendation is **A**. Does that match your instinct, or do you want to weigh B/C further before I write up the full design?

> AGENT

Here's the full design. I'll go section by section.

## Section 1 — The primitive

A new pure, `hass`-free class in a new `resilience.py`:

```python
class CircuitBreaker:
    def __init__(self, threshold, *, window=None,
                 backoff_initial=None, backoff_max=None): ...

    # mutation
    def record_failure(self) -> None        # increments; resets count if windowed & window elapsed
    def record_success(self) -> None         # alias reset()
    def reset(self) -> None

    # reads
    @property failure_count -> int
    @property tripped -> bool                # failure_count >= threshold
    @property backoff_delay -> timedelta     # 0 if not tripped, else min(initial*2**(n-threshold), max)
    def next_attempt_at(self) -> float       # last_failure monotonic + backoff_delay
```

Two configs, one type:

| Scope | Config | `tripped` used for | `backoff_delay` used for | Reset by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Lock** (connectivity) | `threshold=3, backoff_initial=60s, backoff_max=1800s`, no window | gate sync (pause ticks) | the coordinator's poll interval | next poll/push success |
| **Slot** (code won't converge) | `threshold=3, window=5min`, no backoff | suspend that slot + repair issue | unused | slot reaches in-sync |

This is exactly the existing `BACKOFF_*` math (`coordinator.py:128`) and the existing N-in-window logic (`sync.py:456`), lifted verbatim into one place.

## Section 2 — Mapping and deletions

**Coordinator** owns one lock-level breaker. Deleted: `_consecutive_failures`, `_apply_backoff` (becomes `breaker.record_failure()` + interval recompute), `_slot_sync_mgrs_suspended`, the `slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` property, and `suspend_slot_sync_mgrs()`. `_reset_backoff` shrinks to `breaker.reset()` + clear `lock_offline` issue. New read for sync mgrs: `coordinator.unreachable -> breaker.tripped`.

**SlotSyncManager** owns one slot-level breaker. Deleted: `_sync_attempt_count`, `_sync_attempt_first`, `_record_sync_attempt`, `_sync_attempts_exceeded`, `_reset_sync_tracker`. The tick reads `coordinator.unreachable` (instead of `slot_sync_mgrs_suspended`) to enter SUSPENDED, and reads its own `slot_breaker.tripped` for the "code won't converge" suspend.

## Section 3 — Behavior changes (the part I most want your sign-off on)

Three intentional changes, all in the "strictly better" direction but worth confirming:

1. **Connectivity now pauses the sync tick.** Today a disconnected lock backs off polling to 30 min but the 2s sync tick keeps firing and failing fast. New: when the lock breaker trips (3 consecutive connectivity failures), sync managers go SUSPENDED until a poll/push succeeds. *This is the missing coupling — the main reason the gap exists today.*

2. **"Code won't converge" isolates to the slot, not the lock.** Today `sync.py:416` suspends *every* slot on the lock for one bad PIN. New: only that lock+slot suspends. The `slot_suspended` repair issue moves from per-lock scope to per-(lock+slot) scope in `repairs.py`/`strings.json`.

3. **Set-side connectivity failures feed the lock breaker.** Today only polls drive backoff. New: a `LockDisconnected`/`LockOperationFailed` during a sync *set* also counts toward the lock breaker, so "lock is down" converges from both code paths instead of only polling. (This is optional — I can keep set failures purely "retry next tick" if you'd rather not couple them. But it's what makes #1 trip promptly.)

**Out of scope (unchanged):** the operation rate-limiter `_execute_rate_limited` (#1) — it's self-contained and not part of the tangle. The drift timer just swaps its `in backoff` check to read `breaker.tripped`.

## Section 4 — Change plan and testing

- **New** `resilience.py`: `CircuitBreaker` + the `BACKOFF_*`/`MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS`/`SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW` constants relocated from `const.py`.
- **`coordinator.py`**: hold lock breaker; `record_failure`/`reset` at the existing poll/drift/push sites; derive `update_interval`; expose `unreachable`.
- **`sync.py`**: hold slot breaker; replace tracker calls; read `coordinator.unreachable`; route unexpected-exception to slot-level suspend.
- **`repairs.py` / `strings.json` / `translations/en.json`**: re-scope `slot_suspended` to per-slot.
- **Tests**: new `test_resilience.py` with pure synchronous unit tests for both configs (threshold, window expiry, backoff cap, reset) — none of which are directly testable today — plus updated sync/coordinator tests for the re-scoped suspend and the new connectivity→sync coupling.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This refactor changes a *count*: today "is this lock failing?" is answered by reading three fields that three different methods mutate on three different schedules (poll loop, 2s tick, exception handlers). After this, it's one object with one `tripped`. The bugs that hide in scattered-state designs are the **contradictory intermediate states** — e.g. poll backoff active but suspend flag clear, so polling thinks the lock is down while sync thinks it's fine. Collapsing to one source of truth makes those states unrepresentable, which is the same payoff your `SyncState` enum bought when it replaced the old `_dirty`/`_in_sync`/`_tick_in_sync` flags.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Does this look right? In particular: are you on board with all three behavior changes in Section 3, or do you want #3 (set-side failures feeding the lock breaker) left out?

{
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-28-resilience-primitives-consolidation-design.md",
  "content": "# Resilience Primitives Consolidation\n\n**Date**: 2026-05-28\n\n## Problem\n\nRetry, backoff, rate limiting, circuit breaking, and suspension are spread across\n`sync.py`, `coordinator.py`, and `providers/_base.py` as seven distinct mechanisms.\nThe conceptual problem \"is this lock failing?\" is fragmented into **three independent\npieces of state** mutated by three different code paths on three different schedules:\n\n1. **Poll backoff** — `_consecutive_failures` on the coordinator, escalated in\n   `_apply_backoff` (`coordinator.py:125`), reset in `_reset_backoff`.\n2. **Sync circuit breaker** — `_sync_attempt_count` / `_sync_attempt_first` on each\n   `SlotSyncManager`, counted in `_record_sync_attempt` (`sync.py:448`).\n3. **Per-lock suspend flag** — `_slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` on the coordinator, the\n   actual gate that pauses sync.\n\nTwo consequences follow:\n\n- **Contradictory intermediate states are representable.** Poll backoff can be active\n  (coordinator believes the lock is down) while the suspend flag is clear (sync\n  believes the lock is fine), because nothing couples them.\n- **A real coupling is missing.** A disconnected lock backs off polling to 30 minutes,\n  but the 2-second sync tick keeps firing and failing fast at the connection check in\n  `_execute_rate_limited`. The suspend flag is only ever set by the sync circuit breaker\n  or an unexpected exception — never by plain disconnection — so the sync tick is never\n  told \"this lock is down, stop trying.\"\n\nAdditionally, a single non-converging PIN on one slot calls `suspend_slot_sync_mgrs()`\nand freezes syncing for the **entire lock** (`sync.py:416`), even though the failure is\nslot-specific.\n\n## Solution Overview\n\nIntroduce one reusable `CircuitBreaker` value type and instantiate it at two scopes\nwith different policies:\n\n- **Lock-level** (connectivity): owned by the coordinator. Drives the poll interval and\n  gates all sync managers for the lock.\n- **Slot-level** (code won't converge): owned by each `SlotSyncManager`. Suspends only\n  that lock+slot and surfaces a repair issue.\n\nThis collapses the three failure-tracking signals into a single source of truth per\nscope, removes the contradictory-state class of bug, and supplies the missing\nconnectivity→sync coupling.\n\nThe operation rate-limiter (`_execute_rate_limited`, serialize + 2s spacing per lock) is\n**out of scope** — it is self-contained and not part of the tangle.\n\n## The Primitive\n\nA new pure, `hass`-free class in `resilience.py`:\n\n```python\nclass CircuitBreaker:\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        threshold: int,\n        *,\n        window: timedelta | None = None,\n        backoff_initial: timedelta | None = None,\n        backoff_max: timedelta | None = None,\n    ) -> None: ...\n\n    # mutation\n    def record_failure(self) -> None   # increment; if windowed and window elapsed, reset count first\n    def record_success(self) -> None   # alias for reset()\n    def reset(self) -> None\n\n    # reads\n    @property\n    def failure_count(self) -> int: ...\n    @property\n    def tripped(self) -> bool: ...           # failure_count >= threshold\n    @property\n    def backoff_delay(self) -> timedelta: ...  # 0 if not tripped, else min(initial*2**(n-threshold), max)\n    def next_attempt_at(self) -> float: ...    # last_failure (monotonic) + backoff_delay seconds\n```\n\n### Window semantics\n\n`window` only affects **counting** at `record_failure` time: if the window has elapsed\nsince the first failure, the count resets before incrementing. Once `failure_count`\nreaches `threshold`, `tripped` stays `True` until `reset()` is called explicitly. This\ngives the slot-level breaker its \"latch until the slot converges\" behavior while the\nlock-level breaker (no window) counts consecutive failures.\n\n### Backoff semantics\n\n`backoff_delay` reproduces the existing math exactly: `0` until `tripped`, then\n`min(backoff_initial * 2 ** (failure_count - threshold), backoff_max)`. At exactly\n`threshold` failures this yields `backoff_initial` (matching today's `60s` at 3 failures).\n\n## Configuration\n\n| Scope | Config | `tripped` drives | `backoff_delay` drives | Reset by |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Lock** (connectivity) | `threshold=3, backoff_initial=60s, backoff_max=1800s`, no window | pausing all sync managers for the lock | the coordinator poll interval | next successful poll or push |\n| **Slot** (code won't converge) | `threshold=3, window=5min`, no backoff | suspending that lock+slot + repair issue | unused | the slot reaching in-sync |\n\nThese are the existing `BACKOFF_*` values (`coordinator.py`) and the existing\n`MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS` / `SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW` values (`sync.py`), relocated unchanged.\n\n## Mapping onto the coordinator (lock-level)\n\nThe coordinator owns one lock-level `CircuitBreaker`.\n\n**Deleted:** `_consecutive_failures`, `_apply_backoff` (the backoff math moves into the\nbreaker), `_slot_sync_mgrs_suspended`, the `slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` property, and\n`suspend_slot_sync_mgrs()`.\n\n**Replaced:**\n\n- `_apply_backoff()` → `breaker.record_failure()` followed by recomputing\n  `self.update_interval` (= `_original_update_interval` when not tripped, else\n  `breaker.backoff_delay`). The `lock_offline` repair issue at the 12-failure alert\n  threshold is retained, keyed on `breaker.failure_count`.\n- `_reset_backoff()` → `breaker.reset()`, restore `_original_update_interval`, and clear\n  the `lock_offline` issue (kept unconditional so persisted issues clear after restart).\n- New read for sync managers: `coordinator.unreachable` → `breaker.tripped`. This\n  replaces `slot_sync_mgrs_suspended`.\n\n**Failure / success sites (unchanged locations):**\n\n- `async_get_usercodes` failure → `record_failure`; success → `reset`.\n- `_async_drift_check` failure → `record_failure`; the existing \"skip if in backoff\"\n  guard reads `breaker.tripped`.\n- `push_update` → `reset`.\n- **New:** a `LockDisconnected` / `LockOperationFailed` raised during a sync set/clear\n  also calls `record_failure` on the lock breaker (see Behavior Change 3).\n\nWhen the lock breaker resets, the coordinator's existing listener notification drives\n`_request_sync_check` on all sync managers, which transitions them out of SUSPENDED.\n\n## Mapping onto SlotSyncManager (slot-level)\n\nEach `SlotSyncManager` owns one slot-level `CircuitBreaker`.\n\n**Deleted:** `_sync_attempt_count`, `_sync_attempt_first`, `_record_sync_attempt`,\n`_sync_attempts_exceeded`, `_reset_sync_tracker`.\n\n**Replaced:**\n\n- `_record_sync_attempt()` → `slot_breaker.record_failure()` on each set attempt;\n  `slot_breaker.reset()` when the slot reaches in-sync (the existing reset points where\n  `_reset_sync_tracker` was called on convergence).\n- `_sync_attempts_exceeded()` → `slot_breaker.tripped`.\n- The tick's `self._coordinator.slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` check → `coordinator.unreachable`.\n\n**Suspend routing:**\n\n- Slot breaker tripped (\"code won't converge\") → this lock+slot transitions to SUSPENDED\n  and raises the slot-scoped `slot_suspended` repair issue. It no longer touches other\n  slots on the lock.\n- Unexpected exception during sync → slot-level suspend (same SUSPENDED + repair path),\n  isolating to the slot rather than the whole lock.\n\nThe `SyncState` enum and its transitions are otherwise unchanged. SUSPENDED is now\nentered for two reasons — lock unreachable (read from `coordinator.unreachable`) or this\nslot's breaker tripping — and exited the same way as today (via `_request_sync_check`\nonce the gating condition clears).\n\n## Behavior Changes\n\nAll three were reviewed and approved. Each moves behavior in the \"strictly better\"\ndirection.\n\n1. **Connectivity pauses the sync tick.** When the lock breaker trips (3 consecutive\n   connectivity failures), sync managers enter SUSPENDED until a poll or push succeeds,\n   instead of ticking every 2 seconds and failing fast. This is the missing coupling.\n\n2. **\"Code won't converge\" isolates to the slot.** A non-converging PIN suspends only\n   that lock+slot, not every slot on the lock. The `slot_suspended` repair issue is\n   re-scoped from per-lock (`slot_suspended_{entry_id}_{lock_entity_id}`) to\n   per-slot (`slot_suspended_{entry_id}_{lock_entity_id}_{slot_num}`).\n\n3. **Set-side connectivity failures feed the lock breaker.** A `LockDisconnected` /\n   `LockOperationFailed` during a sync set/clear now counts toward the lock breaker, so\n   \"lock is down\" converges from both the poll path and the sync path rather than from\n   polling alone. This is what lets Behavior Change 1 trip promptly.\n\n## Files Changed\n\n1. **`resilience.py`** (new): `CircuitBreaker` class plus the relocated `BACKOFF_*`,\n   `MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS`, and `SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW` constants.\n2. **`const.py`**: remove the relocated constants (or re-export if other modules import\n   them; prefer moving the imports).\n3. **`coordinator.py`**: own the lock-level breaker; call `record_failure` / `reset` at\n   the existing poll, drift, and push sites plus the new set-side site; derive\n   `update_interval` from the breaker; expose `unreachable`; remove the suspend flag and\n   `suspend_slot_sync_mgrs`.\n4. **`sync.py`**: own the slot-level breaker; replace the attempt-tracker calls; read\n   `coordinator.unreachable`; route the unexpected-exception path to slot-level suspend.\n5. **`repairs.py`**: handle the re-scoped per-slot `slot_suspended` issue key.\n6. **`strings.json` / `translations/en.json`**: update the `slot_suspended` translation\n   for per-slot scope.\n\n## Testing\n\n- **`test_resilience.py`** (new): pure synchronous unit tests for `CircuitBreaker` in\n  both configurations — threshold trip, window expiry resetting the count, backoff\n  escalation and cap, `next_attempt_at`, and `reset`. None of this logic is directly\n  unit-testable today because it is buried in async HA callbacks.\n- **Coordinator tests**: poll backoff escalation/cap and recovery now assert through the\n  breaker; verify `unreachable` reflects connectivity.\n- **Sync tests**: the slot-level suspend now isolates to one slot (other slots keep\n  syncing); the new connectivity→sync coupling (lock breaker trip → SUSPENDED, recovery\n  → resume); unexpected exception suspends only the slot.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- `_execute_rate_limited` operation pacing (serialize + 2s spacing). Unchanged.\n- The connection-check timer and drift timer themselves; the drift timer only swaps its\n  \"in backoff\" guard to read `breaker.tripped`.\n"
}

398	async def test_backoff_failure_counter_increments(
399	    poll_coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,
400	    poll_lock: MockLCMLock,
401	) -> None:
402	    """Test that consecutive failure counter increments on each failure."""
403	    # last_update_success=True is required for UpdateFailed to be raised on next failure.
404	    poll_coordinator.last_update_success = True
405	
406	    mock_get = AsyncMock(side_effect=LockDisconnected("Lock offline"))
407	    with patch.object(poll_lock, "async_internal_get_usercodes", mock_get):
408	        for i in range(1, 4):
409	            with pytest.raises(UpdateFailed):
410	                await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()
411	            assert poll_coordinator._consecutive_failures == i
412	
413

415	    poll_coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,
416	    poll_lock: MockLCMLock,
417	) -> None:
418	    """Test that first failure returns empty dict when no prior success."""
419	    # No successful update yet
420	    poll_coordinator.last_update_success = False
421	
422	    mock_get = AsyncMock(side_effect=LockDisconnected("Lock offline"))
423	    with patch.object(poll_lock, "async_internal_get_usercodes", mock_get):
424	        result = await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()
425	
426	    assert result == {}
427	    assert poll_coordinator._consecutive_failures == 1
428	
429

433	) -> None:
434	    """Test that subsequent failures raise UpdateFailed after prior success."""
435	    poll_coordinator.last_update_success = True
436	
437	    mock_get = AsyncMock(side_effect=LockDisconnected("Lock offline"))
438	    with patch.object(poll_lock, "async_internal_get_usercodes", mock_get):
439	        with pytest.raises(UpdateFailed):
440	            await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()
441	
442	    assert poll_coordinator._consecutive_failures == 1
