```
444	
445	async def test_backoff_activates_after_threshold(
446	    poll_coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,
447	    poll_lock: MockLCMLock,
448	) -> None:
449	    """Test that backoff activates after BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD failures."""
450	    original_interval = poll_coordinator.update_interval
451	    poll_coordinator.last_update_success = True
452	
453	    mock_get = AsyncMock(side_effect=LockDisconnected("Lock offline"))
454	    with patch.object(poll_lock, "async_internal_get_usercodes", mock_get):
455	        # Failures below threshold should not change interval
456	        for _ in range(BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD - 1):
457	            with pytest.raises(UpdateFailed):
458	                await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()
459	
460	        assert poll_coordinator.update_interval == original_interval
461	
462	        # Failure at threshold should activate backoff
463	        with pytest.raises(UpdateFailed):
464	            await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()
465	
466	        assert poll_coordinator._consecutive_failures == BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD
467	        expected_backoff = timedelta(
468	            seconds=BACKOFF_INITIAL_SECONDS * 2**0  # 2^(3-3) = 1
469	        )
470	        assert poll_coordinator.update_interval == expected_backoff
471	
472	
473	async def test_backoff_interval_increases_exponentially(
474	    poll_coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,
475	    poll_lock: MockLCMLock,
476	) -> None:
477	    """Test that update_interval increases exponentially for poll-based providers."""
478	    poll_coordinator.last_update_success = True
479	
480	    mock_get = AsyncMock(side_effect=LockDisconnected("Lock offline"))
481	    with patch.object(poll_lock, "async_internal_get_usercodes", mock_get):
482	        # Reach threshold + additional failures
483	        for _ in range(BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD + 3):
484	            with pytest.raises(UpdateFailed):
485	                await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()
486	
487	    # After threshold+3 failures, exponent = 3, backoff = 60 * 2^3 = 480s
488	    expected_backoff = timedelta(seconds=BACKOFF_INITIAL_SECONDS * 2**3)
489	    assert poll_coordinator.update_interval == expected_backoff
490
```

```
10	class AcknowledgeRepairFlow(RepairsFlow):
11	    """Simple repair flow that just acknowledges the issue."""
12	
13	    async def async_step_init(
14	        self, user_input: dict[str, str] | None = None
15	    ) -> data_entry_flow.FlowResult:
16	        """Handle the confirm step."""
17	        if user_input is not None:
18	            return self.async_create_entry(title="", data={})
19	        return self.async_show_form(step_id="init")
20	
21	
22	async def async_create_fix_flow(
23	    hass: HomeAssistant, issue_id: str, data: dict[str, str] | None
24	) -> RepairsFlow:
25	    """Create a fix flow for a repair issue."""
26	    if issue_id.startswith(
27	        ("number_of_uses_removed", "slot_disabled_", "pin_required_", "slot_suspended_")
28	    ):
29	        return AcknowledgeRepairFlow()
30	    raise ValueError(f"Unknown issue: {issue_id}")
```

> AGENT

`pytest-homeassistant-custom-component` ships a `freezer` fixture for the windowed test, and pure tests use plain non-async functions. I have everything.

One design refinement while writing the plan: keeping the `BACKOFF_*`/`MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS`/`SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW` values in `const.py` and passing them into `CircuitBreaker(...)` at construction is simpler than relocating them — `CircuitBreaker` stays config-agnostic, and I avoid churning ~27 test imports. Let me sync the spec to that decision first.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-28-resilience-primitives-consolidation.md",
  "content": "# Resilience Primitives Consolidation Implementation Plan\n\n> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.\n\n**Goal:** Replace the three scattered \"lock is failing\" signals (poll backoff counter, sync attempt counter, per-lock suspend flag) with one reusable `CircuitBreaker` primitive instantiated at two scopes.\n\n**Architecture:** A pure, `hass`-free `CircuitBreaker` class lives in `resilience.py`. The coordinator owns one lock-level instance (connectivity → poll backoff + a `unreachable` gate); each `SlotSyncManager` owns one slot-level instance (\"code won't converge\" → suspend that slot). Concrete thresholds stay in `const.py` and are passed at construction.\n\n**Tech Stack:** Python 3.13, Home Assistant custom integration, pytest (`uv run pytest`, `asyncio_mode = \"auto\"`), `freezer` fixture from `pytest-homeassistant-custom-component`.\n\n**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-28-resilience-primitives-consolidation-design.md`\n\n**Conventions for every task:** No inline/local imports; no `nonlocal`/`global`; comprehensions over loops where natural; docstrings as prose (no Args/Returns sections); spell out acronyms in comments. Run `prek` before each commit. Plans/specs under `docs/` are gitignored — do not commit them; commit only source and tests.\n\n---\n\n## File Structure\n\n- **Create** `custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py` — the `CircuitBreaker` primitive (only).\n- **Create** `tests/test_resilience.py` — pure synchronous unit tests for the primitive.\n- **Modify** `custom_components/lock_code_manager/coordinator.py` — own the lock-level breaker; expose `unreachable`; add `note_connectivity_failure`; remove the suspend flag.\n- **Modify** `custom_components/lock_code_manager/sync.py` — own the slot-level breaker; read `coordinator.unreachable`; isolate suspend to the slot.\n- **Modify** `tests/test_coordinator.py` — update assertions that read `_consecutive_failures`; add `unreachable` tests.\n- **Modify** `tests/test_sync.py` — slot-isolation and connectivity-coupling tests.\n- **Modify** `custom_components/lock_code_manager/strings.json` and `translations/en.json` — re-scope `slot_suspended` title to the slot.\n\n---\n\n## Task 1: `CircuitBreaker` primitive\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py`\n- Test: `tests/test_resilience.py`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**\n\nCreate `tests/test_resilience.py`:\n\n```python\n\"\"\"Tests for the CircuitBreaker resilience primitive.\"\"\"\n\nfrom datetime import timedelta\n\nfrom custom_components.lock_code_manager.resilience import CircuitBreaker\n\n\nclass TestConsecutiveBackoffPolicy:\n    \"\"\"Lock-level config: threshold + exponential backoff, no window.\"\"\"\n\n    def _breaker(self) -> CircuitBreaker:\n        return CircuitBreaker(\n            3,\n            backoff_initial=timedelta(seconds=60),\n            backoff_max=timedelta(seconds=1800),\n        )\n\n    def test_not_tripped_below_threshold(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(2):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 2\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(0)\n\n    def test_tripped_at_threshold_uses_initial_delay(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        # 60 * 2 ** (3 - 3) == 60\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(seconds=60)\n\n    def test_backoff_escalates_exponentially(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(6):  # threshold + 3\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        # 60 * 2 ** (6 - 3) == 480\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(seconds=480)\n\n    def test_backoff_caps_at_max(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(30):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(seconds=1800)\n\n    def test_reset_clears_state(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(5):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.reset()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 0\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(0)\n\n    def test_record_success_is_reset(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.record_success()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 0\n\n\nclass TestWindowedTripPolicy:\n    \"\"\"Slot-level config: threshold within a sliding window, no backoff.\"\"\"\n\n    def _breaker(self) -> CircuitBreaker:\n        return CircuitBreaker(3, window=timedelta(minutes=5))\n\n    def test_trips_at_threshold_within_window(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n\n    def test_backoff_delay_unused_is_zero(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(0)\n\n    def test_window_expiry_resets_count(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=5, seconds=1))\n        # First failure is now outside the window: count restarts at 1.\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 1\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n\n    def test_failures_within_window_accumulate(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=1))\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=1))\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n\n    def test_tripped_latches_until_reset(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        # Even after the window elapses, tripped stays True until reset()\n        # (reads do not re-evaluate the window — only record_failure does).\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=10))\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        breaker.reset()\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Run tests to verify they fail**\n\nRun: `uv run pytest tests/test_resilience.py -v`\nExpected: collection/import error — `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'custom_components.lock_code_manager.resilience'`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the primitive**\n\nCreate `custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py`:\n\n```python\n\"\"\"Reusable circuit breaker primitive for resilience tracking.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta\n\nfrom homeassistant.util import dt as dt_util\n\n\nclass CircuitBreaker:\n    \"\"\"\n    Track failures and decide when to stop or back off.\n\n    A single primitive covers two policies, chosen by the constructor\n    arguments:\n\n    Consecutive backoff (set ``backoff_initial`` / ``backoff_max``, leave\n    ``window`` unset): counts consecutive failures and escalates\n    ``backoff_delay`` exponentially once ``tripped``. Used at the lock level\n    for connectivity.\n\n    Windowed trip (set ``window``, leave the backoff arguments unset): counts\n    failures within a sliding window. ``tripped`` latches once the threshold is\n    reached and stays latched until ``reset`` is called, so a caller decides\n    when recovery happens. Used at the slot level for a code that never\n    converges.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        threshold: int,\n        *,\n        window: timedelta | None = None,\n        backoff_initial: timedelta | None = None,\n        backoff_max: timedelta | None = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Initialize the circuit breaker.\"\"\"\n        self._threshold = threshold\n        self._window = window\n        self._backoff_initial = backoff_initial\n        self._backoff_max = backoff_max\n        self._failure_count = 0\n        self._first_failure: datetime | None = None\n\n    def record_failure(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Record a failure, restarting the count if the window has elapsed.\"\"\"\n        now = dt_util.utcnow()\n        if (\n            self._window is not None\n            and self._first_failure is not None\n            and now - self._first_failure > self._window\n        ):\n            self._failure_count = 0\n            self._first_failure = None\n        if self._first_failure is None:\n            self._first_failure = now\n        self._failure_count += 1\n\n    def record_success(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Record a success, clearing all failure state.\"\"\"\n        self.reset()\n\n    def reset(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Clear all failure state.\"\"\"\n        self._failure_count = 0\n        self._first_failure = None\n\n    @property\n    def failure_count(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the current failure count.\"\"\"\n        return self._failure_count\n\n    @property\n    def tripped(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return whether the failure threshold has been reached.\"\"\"\n        return self._failure_count >= self._threshold\n\n    @property\n    def backoff_delay(self) -> timedelta:\n        \"\"\"Return the current backoff delay, or zero when not tripped.\"\"\"\n        if not self.tripped or self._backoff_initial is None:\n            return timedelta(0)\n        delay = self._backoff_initial * 2 ** (self._failure_count - self._threshold)\n        if self._backoff_max is not None and delay > self._backoff_max:\n            return self._backoff_max\n        return delay\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `uv run pytest tests/test_resilience.py -v`\nExpected: all PASS.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py tests/test_resilience.py\ngit commit -m \"feat: add CircuitBreaker resilience primitive\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 2: Coordinator adopts the lock-level breaker\n\nReplaces `_consecutive_failures` with a `CircuitBreaker`, keeps backoff behavior identical, and adds the `unreachable` gate that the sync layer will read in Task 3. Plan complete and saved (local only, not committed) to:

`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-28-resilience-primitives-consolidation.md`

It's five TDD tasks, each ending green and committable:

1. **`CircuitBreaker` primitive** — new `resilience.py` + pure synchronous unit tests (threshold, exponential backoff + cap, windowed reset, latch-until-reset).
2. **Coordinator** — adopt the lock-level breaker for poll backoff; add the `unreachable` gate; keep behavior identical.
3. **SlotSyncManager** — adopt the slot-level breaker; read `coordinator.unreachable`; isolate suspension to the single slot (Behavior Changes 1 & 2); remove the old suspend flag from the coordinator.
4. **Connectivity coupling** — feed set/clear transport failures into the lock breaker (Behavior Change 3).
5. **Repair strings** — re-scope the `slot_suspended` issue title to the slot; full-suite + `prek` gate.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The task ordering is built around keeping every commit green despite a two-way dependency: today `sync.py` both *sets* the suspend flag (`suspend_slot_sync_mgrs`) and *reads* it (`slot_sync_mgrs_suspended`). You can't delete that flag from the coordinator until sync stops touching it. So Task 2 deliberately leaves the flag in place, and Task 3 removes the flag and rewires sync in the same commit. That's why Task 3 is the one task that legitimately spans two files — splitting it would leave a red commit in between.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Two execution options:

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** — I dispatch a fresh subagent per task and review between tasks. Fast iteration, and it keeps each task's context clean. Note: Tasks 3–4 reference existing `test_sync.py` fixtures/patching helpers that the executor must read and reuse (flagged in the plan's self-review notes).

**2. Inline Execution** — I execute the tasks in this session with checkpoints for your review.

Which approach would you like?

> DEVELOPER

is this going to increase or reduce number of lines of code?

> AGENT

Honest answer: raw line count will **go up**, not down. The complexity goes down, but that's a different metric. Let me give you the actual numbers from the plan rather than hand-wave.

Estimated net change, **source code only**:

| File | Removed | Added | Net |
|---|---|---|---|
| `resilience.py` (new) | — | ~92 | **+92** |
| `coordinator.py` | `suspend_slot_sync_mgrs` (14), `slot_sync_mgrs_suspended` prop (4), 2 fields, slimmer backoff bodies | `unreachable` (5), `note_connectivity_failure` (8), breaker ctor (5) | **~−11** |
| `sync.py` | `_record_sync_attempt` (19), `_sync_attempts_exceeded` (7), `_reset_sync_tracker` (4) | breaker ctor + per-slot key bits | **~−26** |
| **Source total** | | | **~+55** |

Plus tests: a new `test_resilience.py` (~120 lines) and a handful of new coordinator/sync tests (~60), so **~+180 test lines**.

So: **~+55 source, ~+180 test.** Net more lines overall.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This is the usual shape of a "consolidation" refactor: LOC goes *up* while complexity goes *down*. The reductions here aren't line-shaped — they're:
- **State variables:** 3 scattered failure signals (`_consecutive_failures`, `_sync_attempt_count`/`_first`, `_slot_sync_mgrs_suspended`) → 2 well-scoped breaker instances. Fewer mutable fields = fewer representable contradictory states.
- **Reset paths:** the hand-bridged "`_reset_backoff` also clears the suspend flag" coupling disappears into one `reset()`.
- **Testability:** ~90 of the new lines are a *standalone, synchronously testable* class (plus its docstrings). Today that same logic is untestable except by driving the HA event loop — which is exactly why the +180 test lines didn't exist before.

LOC is a poor proxy for "complexity" here; the metrics that actually predict maintenance cost (state count, coupling, branch density per file, % logic under direct unit test) all improve.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

If your real constraint is **fewer total lines** rather than lower complexity, the calculus changes and I'd steer differently — e.g. drop the new test file, or skip `resilience.py` and just extract two small helper functions in place (the Approach C we set aside, which nets closer to flat).

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Reusable circuit breaker primitive for resilience tracking.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta\n\nfrom homeassistant.util import dt as dt_util\n\n\nclass CircuitBreaker:\n    \"\"\"\n    Track failures and decide when to stop or back off.\n\n    A single primitive covers two policies, chosen by the constructor\n    arguments:\n\n    Consecutive backoff (set ``backoff_initial`` / ``backoff_max``, leave\n    ``window`` unset): counts consecutive failures and escalates\n    ``backoff_delay`` exponentially once ``tripped``. Used at the lock level\n    for connectivity.\n\n    Windowed trip (set ``window``, leave the backoff arguments unset): counts\n    failures within a sliding window. ``tripped`` latches once the threshold is\n    reached and stays latched until ``reset`` is called, so a caller decides\n    when recovery happens. Used at the slot level for a code that never\n    converges.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        threshold: int,\n        *,\n        window: timedelta | None = None,\n        backoff_initial: timedelta | None = None,\n        backoff_max: timedelta | None = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Initialize the circuit breaker.\"\"\"\n        self._threshold = threshold\n        self._window = window\n        self._backoff_initial = backoff_initial\n        self._backoff_max = backoff_max\n        self._failure_count = 0\n        self._first_failure: datetime | None = None\n\n    def record_failure(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Record a failure, restarting the count if the window has elapsed.\"\"\"\n        now = dt_util.utcnow()\n        if (\n            self._window is not None\n            and self._first_failure is not None\n            and now - self._first_failure > self._window\n        ):\n            self._failure_count = 0\n            self._first_failure = None\n        if self._first_failure is None:\n            self._first_failure = now\n        self._failure_count += 1\n\n    def record_success(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Record a success, clearing all failure state.\"\"\"\n        self.reset()\n\n    def reset(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Clear all failure state.\"\"\"\n        self._failure_count = 0\n        self._first_failure = None\n\n    @property\n    def failure_count(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the current failure count.\"\"\"\n        return self._failure_count\n\n    @property\n    def tripped(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return whether the failure threshold has been reached.\"\"\"\n        return self._failure_count >= self._threshold\n\n    @property\n    def backoff_delay(self) -> timedelta:\n        \"\"\"Return the current backoff delay, or zero when not tripped.\"\"\"\n        if not self.tripped or self._backoff_initial is None:\n            return timedelta(0)\n        delay = self._backoff_initial * 2 ** (self._failure_count - self._threshold)\n        if self._backoff_max is not None and delay > self._backoff_max:\n            return self._backoff_max\n        return delay\n"
}
```

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for the CircuitBreaker resilience primitive.\"\"\"\n\nfrom datetime import timedelta\n\nfrom custom_components.lock_code_manager.resilience import CircuitBreaker\n\n\nclass TestConsecutiveBackoffPolicy:\n    \"\"\"Lock-level config: threshold + exponential backoff, no window.\"\"\"\n\n    def _breaker(self) -> CircuitBreaker:\n        return CircuitBreaker(\n            3,\n            backoff_initial=timedelta(seconds=60),\n            backoff_max=timedelta(seconds=1800),\n        )\n\n    def test_not_tripped_below_threshold(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(2):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 2\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(0)\n\n    def test_tripped_at_threshold_uses_initial_delay(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        # 60 * 2 ** (3 - 3) == 60\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(seconds=60)\n\n    def test_backoff_escalates_exponentially(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(6):  # threshold + 3\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        # 60 * 2 ** (6 - 3) == 480\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(seconds=480)\n\n    def test_backoff_caps_at_max(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(30):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(seconds=1800)\n\n    def test_reset_clears_state(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(5):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.reset()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 0\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(0)\n\n    def test_record_success_is_reset(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.record_success()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 0\n\n\nclass TestWindowedTripPolicy:\n    \"\"\"Slot-level config: threshold within a sliding window, no backoff.\"\"\"\n\n    def _breaker(self) -> CircuitBreaker:\n        return CircuitBreaker(3, window=timedelta(minutes=5))\n\n    def test_trips_at_threshold_within_window(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n\n    def test_backoff_delay_unused_is_zero(self) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.backoff_delay == timedelta(0)\n\n    def test_window_expiry_resets_count(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=5, seconds=1))\n        # First failure is now outside the window: count restarts at 1.\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.failure_count == 1\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n\n    def test_failures_within_window_accumulate(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=1))\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=1))\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n\n    def test_tripped_latches_until_reset(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        # Even after the window elapses, tripped stays True until reset()\n        # (reads do not re-evaluate the window -- only record_failure does).\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=10))\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        breaker.reset()\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "    # Shutdown should NOT delete the issue — it persists across restarts\n    await poll_coordinator.async_shutdown()\n    assert issue_registry.async_get_issue(DOMAIN, issue_id) is not None\n\n\nasync def test_unreachable_reflects_backoff_trip(\n    poll_coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,\n    poll_lock: MockLCMLock,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"unreachable is False until the lock breaker trips, then True.\"\"\"\n    poll_coordinator.last_update_success = True\n    assert poll_coordinator.unreachable is False\n\n    mock_get = AsyncMock(side_effect=LockDisconnected(\"Lock offline\"))\n    with patch.object(poll_lock, \"async_internal_get_usercodes\", mock_get):\n        for _ in range(BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD):\n            with pytest.raises(UpdateFailed):\n                await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()\n\n    assert poll_coordinator.unreachable is True\n\n\nasync def test_unreachable_clears_on_recovery(\n    poll_coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,\n    poll_lock: MockLCMLock,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A successful poll resets the breaker and clears unreachable.\"\"\"\n    poll_coordinator.last_update_success = True\n    mock_get = AsyncMock(side_effect=LockDisconnected(\"Lock offline\"))\n    with patch.object(poll_lock, \"async_internal_get_usercodes\", mock_get):\n        for _ in range(BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD):\n            with pytest.raises(UpdateFailed):\n                await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()\n    assert poll_coordinator.unreachable is True\n\n    mock_get_ok = AsyncMock(return_value={1: \"1234\"})\n    with patch.object(poll_lock, \"async_internal_get_usercodes\", mock_get_ok):\n        await poll_coordinator.async_get_usercodes()\n\n    assert poll_coordinator.unreachable is False\n    assert poll_coordinator.update_interval == poll_coordinator._original_update_interval"
}
```

```
116	            pytest.param(
117	                "1234",
118	                {
119	                    "active": STATE_ON,
120	                    "pin": "1234",
121	                    "coordinator_code": SlotCode.EMPTY,
122	                },
123	                True,
124	                id="active-empty-code-matching-last-set",
125	            ),
126	            pytest.param(
127	                "5678",
128	                {
129	                    "active": STATE_ON,
130	                    "pin": "1234",
131	                    "coordinator_code": SlotCode.EMPTY,
132	                },
133	                False,
134	                id="active-empty-code-mismatched-last-set",
135	            ),
136	            pytest.param(
137	                None,
138	                {
139	                    "active": STATE_ON,
140	                    "pin": "1234",
141	                    "coordinator_code": None,
142	                    "code": "1234",
143	                },
144	                True,
145	                id="active-no-coordinator-data-matching",
146	            ),
147	            pytest.param(
148	                None,
149	                {
150	                    "active": STATE_ON,
151	                    "pin": "1234",
152	                    "coordinator_code": None,
153	                    "code": "5678",
154	                },
155	                False,
156	                id="active-no-coordinator-data-mismatched",
157	            ),
158	            # -- Inactive (OFF) + various lock codes --
159	            pytest.param(
160	                None,
161	                {"active": STATE_OFF, "coordinator_code": SlotCode.EMPTY},
162	                True,
163	                id="inactive-empty-code",
164	            ),
165	            pytest.param(
166	                None,
167	                {"active": STATE_OFF, "coordinator_code": SlotCode.UNREADABLE_CODE},
168	                False,
169	                id="inactive-unknown-code",
170	            ),
171	            pytest.param(
172	                None,
173	                {"active": STATE_OFF, "coordinator_code": "1234"},
174	                False,
175	                id="inactive-has-pin",
176	            ),
177	            pytest.param(
178	                None,
179	                {"active": STATE_OFF, "coordinator_code": None, "code": ""},
180	                True,
181	                id="inactive-empty-string-fallback",
182	            ),
183	            pytest.param(
184	                None,
185	                {"active": STATE_OFF, "coordinator_code": None, "code": "1234"},
186	                False,
187	                id="inactive-nonempty-string-fallback",
188	            ),
189	        ],
190	    )
191	    def test_calculate_in_sync(
192	        self,
193	        last_set_pin: str | None,
194	        slot_kwargs: dict,
195	        expected: bool,
196	    ) -> None:
197	        """Test calculate_in_sync with various active/inactive and code combos."""
198	        assert (
199	            _manager(last_set_pin=last_set_pin).calculate_in_sync(_slot(**slot_kwargs))
200	            is expected
201	        )

204	class TestTryUpgradeStateTracking:
205	    """Tests for upgrading catch-all to targeted state tracking."""
206	
207	    async def test_upgrade_catchall_to_targeted_on_tick(
208	        self,
209	        hass: HomeAssistant,
210	        mock_lock_config_entry,
211	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
212	        caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture,
213	    ) -> None:
214	        """Catch-all tracking upgrades to targeted when entities become available."""
215	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
216	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
217	
218	        # The manager starts in catch-all mode because the code sensor entity
219	        # is not yet registered when _setup_state_tracking runs during setup.
220	        # After full setup completes, the entities are available so a tick
221	        # should upgrade to targeted tracking.
222	        assert manager._tracking_all_states
223	        old_unsub = manager._state_tracking_unsub
224	
225	        with caplog.at_level(logging.DEBUG):
226	            await async_trigger_sync_tick(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
227	
228	        assert not manager._tracking_all_states
229	        assert manager._state_tracking_unsub is not old_unsub
230	        assert manager._state_tracking_unsub is not None
231	
232	    async def test_no_upgrade_when_entities_missing(
233	        self,
234	        hass: HomeAssistant,
235	        mock_lock_config_entry,
236	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
237	    ) -> None:
238	        """Catch-all tracking stays when entities are still missing."""
239	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
240	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
241	
242	        # Force back into catch-all with a mock unsub and clear entity map
243	        catch_all_unsub = MagicMock()
244	        manager._state_tracking_unsub = catch_all_unsub
245	        manager._tracking_all_states = True
246	        manager._entity_id_map.clear()
247	
248	        with patch.object(
249	            manager._ent_reg,
250	            "async_get_entity_id",
251	            return_value=None,
252	        ):
253	            await async_trigger_sync_tick(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
254	
255	        # Should still be in catch-all mode
256	        assert manager._tracking_all_states
257	        catch_all_unsub.assert_not_called()
258	
259	    async def test_no_upgrade_when_already_targeted(
260	        self,
261	        hass: HomeAssistant,
262	        mock_lock_config_entry,
263	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
264	    ) -> None:
265	        """No-op when already using targeted tracking."""
266	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
267	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
268	
269	        # First tick upgrades from catch-all to targeted
270	        await async_trigger_sync_tick(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
271	        assert not manager._tracking_all_states
272	        original_unsub = manager._state_tracking_unsub
273	
274	        # Second tick should be a no-op for state tracking
275	        await async_trigger_sync_tick(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
276	        assert not manager._tracking_all_states
277	        assert manager._state_tracking_unsub is original_unsub

280	class TestDisableSlotExceptionHandling:
281	    """Tests for _disable_slot exception handling (Issue 7)."""
282	
283	    async def test_disable_slot_exception_resets_sync_tracker(
284	        self,
285	        hass: HomeAssistant,
286	        mock_lock_config_entry,
287	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
288	        caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture,
289	    ) -> None:
290	        """Sync tracker resets even when async_disable_slot raises."""
291	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
292	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
293	
294	        # Set up some sync tracker state to verify it gets reset
295	        manager._sync_attempt_count = 5
296	        manager._sync_attempt_first = MagicMock()
297	
298	        with patch(
299	            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.sync.async_disable_slot",
300	            new_callable=AsyncMock,
301	            side_effect=RuntimeError("service call failed"),
302	        ):
303	            await manager._disable_slot("test reason")
304	
305	        # Sync tracker should still be reset even after exception
306	        assert manager._sync_attempt_count == 0
307	        assert manager._sync_attempt_first is None
308	        assert "Failed to disable slot" in caplog.text
309	
310	        # Fallback repair issue should be created even though service call failed
311	        issue_registry = async_get_issue_registry(hass)
312	        entry_id = lock_code_manager_config_entry.entry_id
313	        issue = issue_registry.async_get_issue(DOMAIN, f"slot_disabled_{entry_id}_1")
314	        assert issue is not None
315	        assert issue.severity == IssueSeverity.WARNING
316	
317	    async def test_disable_slot_success_resets_sync_tracker(
318	        self,
319	        hass: HomeAssistant,
320	        mock_lock_config_entry,
321	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
322	    ) -> None:
323	        """Sync tracker resets when async_disable_slot succeeds."""
324	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
325	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
326	
327	        manager._sync_attempt_count = 5
328	        manager._sync_attempt_first = MagicMock()
329	
330	        with patch(
331	            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.sync.async_disable_slot",
332	            new_callable=AsyncMock,
333	            return_value=True,
334	        ):
335	            await manager._disable_slot("test reason")
336	
337	        assert manager._sync_attempt_count == 0
338	        assert manager._sync_attempt_first is None

341	class TestSlotDisabledIssueCleanup:
342	    """Tests for slot_disabled_ issue auto-deletion when back in sync (Issue 1)."""
343	
344	    async def test_slot_disabled_issue_deleted_when_back_in_sync(
345	        self,
346	        hass: HomeAssistant,
347	        mock_lock_config_entry,
348	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
349	    ) -> None:
350	        """slot_disabled_ repair issue is deleted when slot comes back in sync."""
351	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
352	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
353	        entry_id = lock_code_manager_config_entry.entry_id
354	        slot_num = manager._slot_num
355	
356	        # Manually create a slot_disabled issue to simulate circuit breaker
357	        issue_id = f"slot_disabled_{entry_id}_{slot_num}"
358	        async_create_issue(
359	            hass,
360	            DOMAIN,
361	            issue_id,
362	            is_fixable=True,
363	            is_persistent=True,
364	            severity=IssueSeverity.WARNING,
365	            translation_key="slot_disabled",
366	            translation_placeholders={
367	                "slot_num": str(slot_num),
368	                "reason": "test",
369	            },
370	        )
371	
372	        issue_registry = async_get_issue_registry(hass)
373	        assert issue_registry.async_get_issue(DOMAIN, issue_id) is not None
374	
375	        # Initialize the manager state so it thinks it was out of sync
376	        manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
377	
378	        # Trigger a tick that will find the slot in sync (coordinator has matching code)
379	        await async_trigger_sync_tick(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
380	
381	        # The issue should be deleted since slot is back in sync
382	        assert issue_registry.async_get_issue(DOMAIN, issue_id) is None
383

385	class TestLockOperationFailedRetry:
386	    """Tests that LockOperationFailed triggers retry, not slot disable."""
387	
388	    async def test_lock_operation_failed_triggers_retry(
389	        self,
390	        hass: HomeAssistant,
391	        mock_lock_config_entry,
392	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
393	    ) -> None:
394	        """LockOperationFailed during sync sets dirty for retry instead of disabling slot."""
395	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
396	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
397	
398	        # Force out-of-sync state so _perform_sync is called:
399	        # set coordinator data to EMPTY so the slot appears to need a set
400	        manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
401	        manager._coordinator.data[1] = SlotCode.EMPTY
402	
403	        with patch.object(
404	            manager,
405	            "_perform_sync",
406	            new_callable=AsyncMock,
407	            side_effect=LockOperationFailed("service validation failed"),
408	        ):
409	            await manager._async_tick()
410	            await hass.async_block_till_done()
411	
412	        # Should retry (OUT_OF_SYNC) rather than disable the slot
413	        assert manager._state is SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC

416	class TestSyncStateMachine:
417	    """Tests for SyncState transitions in SlotSyncManager."""
418	
419	    async def test_initial_state_is_loading(
420	        self,
421	        hass: HomeAssistant,
422	        mock_lock_config_entry,
423	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
424	    ) -> None:
425	        """Manager starts in LOADING then transitions after a tick resolves entities."""
426	        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)
427	        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager
428	        # The initial tick during async_start may not resolve entities yet
429	        # (they may not be registered), but after full setup + a tick,
430	        # the manager should transition out of LOADING.
431	        await async_trigger_sync_tick(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY, set_dirty=False)
432	        assert manager._state in (SyncState.IN_SYNC, SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC)

682	        lock_code_manager_config_entry,
```

> AGENT

Now I understand the fixtures (SLOT_2 exists on the same lock; `async_trigger_sync_tick_for_manager` is available). 

One design detail the plan under-specified: how a slot-code-suspended slot *recovers*. I traced today's behavior — the lock-wide flag clears on the next successful poll/push (which also fires the coordinator listener → `_request_sync_check`). To preserve that recovery cadence while isolating to the slot, the `_request_sync_check` SUSPENDED branch will reset the slot breaker and go OUT_OF_SYNC when the lock is reachable. That matches today's "retry-per-recovery-cycle" semantics exactly, just scoped to one slot.

Let me make the sync.py source edits.

```json
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/sync.py",
  "old_string": "    # -- Attempt tracking + circuit breaker ----------------------------------\n\n    def _reset_sync_tracker(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Reset the sync attempt tracker.\"\"\"\n        self._sync_attempt_count = 0\n        self._sync_attempt_first = None\n\n    def _record_sync_attempt(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Record a sync attempt toward circuit breaker counter.\n\n        Called for set operations. Successful clear operations and transient\n        LockDisconnected errors are not tracked since they represent\n        transient lock communication issues, not persistent failures.\n        \"\"\"\n        now = dt_util.utcnow()\n        if (\n            self._sync_attempt_first is not None\n            and now - self._sync_attempt_first > SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW\n        ):\n            self._sync_attempt_count = 0\n            self._sync_attempt_first = None\n\n        if self._sync_attempt_first is None:\n            self._sync_attempt_first = now\n        self._sync_attempt_count += 1\n\n    def _sync_attempts_exceeded(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Check if sync attempts exceeded the limit within the time window.\"\"\"\n        if self._sync_attempt_count < MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS:\n            return False\n        if self._sync_attempt_first is None:\n            return False\n        return dt_util.utcnow() - self._sync_attempt_first <= SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW\n\n    # -- Orchestration -------------------------------------------------------",
  "new_string": "    # -- Orchestration -------------------------------------------------------"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/test_sync.py",
  "old_string": "    async def test_suspended_to_out_of_sync_on_coordinator_recovery(\n        self,\n        hass: HomeAssistant,\n        mock_lock_config_entry,\n        lock_code_manager_config_entry,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"SUSPENDED transitions to OUT_OF_SYNC when coordinator clears suspended flag.\"\"\"\n        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)\n        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager\n\n        manager._state = SyncState.SUSPENDED\n        manager._coordinator.suspend_slot_sync_mgrs()\n\n        manager._coordinator._slot_sync_mgrs_suspended = False\n        manager._request_sync_check()\n\n        assert manager._state is SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n\n    async def test_suspended_stays_suspended_when_coordinator_still_suspended(\n        self,\n        hass: HomeAssistant,\n        mock_lock_config_entry,\n        lock_code_manager_config_entry,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"SUSPENDED stays SUSPENDED when coordinator flag is still set.\"\"\"\n        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)\n        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager\n\n        manager._state = SyncState.SUSPENDED\n        manager._coordinator.suspend_slot_sync_mgrs()\n\n        manager._request_sync_check()\n        assert manager._state is SyncState.SUSPENDED",
  "new_string": "    async def test_suspended_to_out_of_sync_on_coordinator_recovery(\n        self,\n        hass: HomeAssistant,\n        mock_lock_config_entry,\n        lock_code_manager_config_entry,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"SUSPENDED transitions to OUT_OF_SYNC when the lock becomes reachable.\"\"\"\n        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)\n        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager\n\n        # Lock unreachable suspends the slot.\n        for _ in range(BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD):\n            manager._coordinator._lock_breaker.record_failure()\n        manager._state = SyncState.SUSPENDED\n\n        # Lock recovers: breaker resets, so the next sync check resumes.\n        manager._coordinator._lock_breaker.reset()\n        manager._request_sync_check()\n\n        assert manager._state is SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n\n    async def test_suspended_stays_suspended_when_lock_still_unreachable(\n        self,\n        hass: HomeAssistant,\n        mock_lock_config_entry,\n        lock_code_manager_config_entry,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"SUSPENDED stays SUSPENDED while the lock is still unreachable.\"\"\"\n        entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)\n        manager = entity_obj._sync_manager\n\n        for _ in range(BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD):\n            manager._coordinator._lock_breaker.record_failure()\n        manager._state = SyncState.SUSPENDED\n\n        manager._request_sync_check()\n        assert manager._state is SyncState.SUSPENDED"
}
```

> AGENT

This surfaced more consumers and a subtle semantic point. The old `_sync_attempts_exceeded()` re-evaluated the window on every read (stale breaches outside the window didn't count). My `tripped` latched instead. To stay faithful to the original slot behavior — and keep the existing window-expiry tests valid — I'll make the windowed `tripped` auto-expire (textbook sliding-window). This is functionally identical in the real flow (the breaker is always reset at suspend), and it's the more correct primitive.

Updating the primitive:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01HLW6cbQtaCMK8MEJBfTN2R
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py",
  "old_string": "    @property\n    def tripped(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return whether the failure threshold has been reached.\"\"\"\n        return self._failure_count >= self._threshold",
  "new_string": "    @property\n    def tripped(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"\n        Return whether the failure threshold has been reached.\n\n        For a windowed breaker the breaches must fall within the trailing\n        window; once the window elapses with no new failures the stale\n        breaches no longer count as tripped.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self._failure_count < self._threshold:\n            return False\n        if self._window is not None and self._first_failure is not None:\n            return dt_util.utcnow() - self._first_failure <= self._window\n        return True"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/test_resilience.py",
  "old_string": "    def test_tripped_latches_until_reset(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        # Even after the window elapses, tripped stays True until reset()\n        # (reads do not re-evaluate the window -- only record_failure does).\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=10))\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        breaker.reset()\n        assert not breaker.tripped",
  "new_string": "    def test_tripped_clears_when_window_elapses(self, freezer) -> None:\n        breaker = self._breaker()\n        for _ in range(3):\n            breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        # Once the window passes with no new failures, the stale breaches no\n        # longer count as tripped.\n        freezer.tick(timedelta(minutes=5, seconds=1))\n        assert not breaker.tripped\n        # reset() also clears the tripped state immediately.\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        breaker.record_failure()\n        assert breaker.tripped\n        breaker.reset()\n        assert not breaker.tripped"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-28-resilience-primitives-consolidation-design.md",
  "old_string": "`window` only affects **counting** at `record_failure` time: if the window has elapsed\nsince the first failure, the count resets before incrementing. Once `failure_count`\nreaches `threshold`, `tripped` stays `True` until `reset()` is called. This\ngives the slot-level breaker its \"latch until the slot converges\" behavior while the\nlock-level breaker (no window) counts consecutive failures.",
  "new_string": "For a windowed breaker, `record_failure` restarts the count when the window has elapsed\nsince the first failure, and `tripped` only reports `True` while the breaches fall\nwithin the trailing window (a textbook sliding window). The lock-level breaker has no\nwindow, so it counts consecutive failures and `tripped` stays `True` until `reset()`.\nIn the real sync flow this distinction is moot — the slot breaker is reset whenever the\nslot is suspended or recovers — but the sliding window keeps a stale count from causing a\nspurious trip on a later, unrelated sync."
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "    # Pre-load the tracker to simulate repeated failures on the same PIN\n    now = dt_util.utcnow()\n    sync_mgr._sync_attempt_count = MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS\n    sync_mgr._sync_attempt_first = now\n\n    # Trigger tick — circuit breaker should fire before attempting sync\n    sync_mgr._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n    await sync_mgr._async_tick()\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    # Lock should be suspended (not slot disabled)\n    assert sync_mgr._state is SyncState.SUSPENDED\n    assert coordinator.slot_sync_mgrs_suspended is True",
  "new_string": "    # Pre-load the breaker to simulate repeated failures on the same PIN\n    for _ in range(MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS):\n        sync_mgr._slot_breaker.record_failure()\n\n    # Trigger tick — circuit breaker should fire before attempting sync\n    sync_mgr._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n    await sync_mgr._async_tick()\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    # Slot should be suspended; the lock itself is not marked unreachable\n    assert sync_mgr._state is SyncState.SUSPENDED\n    assert coordinator.unreachable is False"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "    When the attempt window expires, record_failure resets the counter,\n    preventing stale counts from triggering the breaker on a new sync cycle.\n    \"\"\"\n    await async_initial_tick(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)\n\n    in_sync_entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)\n    sync_mgr = in_sync_entity_obj._sync_manager\n\n    # Verify in sync\n    assert hass.states.get(SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY).state == STATE_ON\n\n    # Simulate prior sync attempts from an EXPIRED window\n    sync_mgr._slot_breaker._failure_count = MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS - 1\n    sync_mgr._slot_breaker._first_failure = dt_util.utcnow() - SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW * 2"
```

```json
  "new_string": "        assert failing._state is SyncState.SUSPENDED\n        # The sibling slot on the same lock is unaffected.\n        assert sibling._state is not SyncState.SUSPENDED\n        # A slot-level failure does not mark the whole lock unreachable.\n        assert failing._coordinator.unreachable is False\n\n    async def test_set_disconnect_failures_trip_lock_breaker(\n        self,\n        hass: HomeAssistant,\n        mock_lock_config_entry,\n        lock_code_manager_config_entry,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Repeated LockDisconnected on set trips the lock breaker and suspends the tick.\"\"\"\n        manager = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY)._sync_manager\n        manager._coordinator.data[1] = SlotCode.EMPTY\n\n        with patch.object(\n            manager,\n            \"_perform_sync\",\n            new_callable=AsyncMock,\n            side_effect=LockDisconnected(\"offline\"),\n        ):\n            for _ in range(BACKOFF_FAILURE_THRESHOLD):\n                manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n                await manager._async_tick()\n                await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n        # Connectivity failures during set converged to \"unreachable\".\n        assert manager._coordinator.unreachable is True\n\n        # The next tick observes the unreachable lock and suspends instead of\n        # retrying every tick.\n        manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n        await manager._async_tick()\n        assert manager._state is SyncState.SUSPENDED\n\n\nclass TestSyncStatusAttribute:"
}
```

```
I have what I need. Here's my analysis.

## Type: CircuitBreaker

### Invariants Identified
- `_failure_count >= 0`; `_first_failure is None` iff `_failure_count == 0` (the two move together in `record_failure`/`reset`).
- `tripped` implies `_failure_count >= _threshold`; for windowed breakers it additionally requires the first failure to be inside the trailing window.
- `backoff_delay` is non-zero only when `tripped` and `_backoff_initial` is set; it grows as `initial * 2^(count - threshold)`, capped at `_backoff_max`.
- Implicit, unenforced: exactly one policy should be configured — `window` XOR (`backoff_initial`/`backoff_max`).

### Ratings
- **Encapsulation**: 6/10
  State is private with read-only properties, no mutable internals leak. But tests reach into `_failure_count`/`_first_failure` for setup, which means the construct-then-mutate path is a de facto second public API. A `record_failure()` loop or a tiny test helper would keep the seam private.

- **Invariant Expression**: 4/10
  This is the weak point. One class serves two policies selected by which optional kwargs you pass, and nothing expresses or enforces that choice. All four config combinations type-check: passing both `window` and `backoff_initial` silently yields a windowed breaker that also backs off; passing neither yields a count-only breaker that trips but never backs off and never forgets. The valid configurations live only in the docstring. The mode is latent, not named.

- **Invariant Usefulness**: 8/10
  The guarantees that do hold are exactly what the two callers need: lock-level gets escalating `backoff_delay`, slot-level gets a latching windowed `tripped`. The count/first-failure coupling prevents stale-window bugs. Genuinely useful behavior.

- **Invariant Enforcement**: 5/10
  Runtime state transitions are consistent and centralized (success delegates to reset, window expiry resets cleanly). But construction does zero validation — the most important invariant (pick one coherent policy) is entirely unenforced. Construction is the natural place to make illegal states unrepresentable, and it's skipped.

### Strengths
Small, focused, immutable-config + minimal-mutable-state shape. Window-expiry logic is correct and the success→reset delegation avoids drift. The property surface (`failure_count`, `tripped`, `backoff_delay`) maps 1:1 to what each consumer reads.

### Concerns
The dual-purpose-via-optional-kwargs design is the core issue: the policy is implicit, and incoherent configs construct happily. This is a maintainability trap more than a live bug (both call sites are correct today).

### Recommended Improvements (low-cost, pick one or two)
1. Add a 3-line `__init__` guard asserting exactly one of `window` / `backoff_initial` is set — cheapest win, closes the worst gap.
2. Optionally expose two classmethods (`CircuitBreaker.windowed(...)` / `.backoff(...)`) so the policy is named at the call site; keep `__init__` private-ish.
3. Don't refactor the test seam aggressively — for an internal helper, poking `_failure_count` is acceptable; a small `_seed(count, first_failure)` test helper would be nicer but isn't worth a breaking change.

For a small internal helper this is solid; the only thing I'd actually do is the constructor guard.

Relevant files: `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py`, `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/coordinator.py` (lines 63-67), `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/sync.py` (lines 164-166).
```

```
Confirmed. Push-only providers have `update_interval = None`, so there's no poll loop to call `_reset_backoff`; recovery depends entirely on `push_update()`. This produces my main finding. Here is the review.

## Error handling review: `refactor/resilience-circuit-breaker`

The refactor is mostly clean: every except block logs and surfaces a repair issue, and state transitions are present on each path. The deliberate `except Exception` safety net is justified and well-implemented. I found a few genuine stuck-state / silent-failure risks, none catastrophic.

**HIGH — Lock breaker tripped purely by sync transport failures can never recover for push-only providers**
`coordinator.py:129-137` (`note_connectivity_failure`) and `sync.py:623-634`. When set/clear transport failures trip the lock breaker, `unreachable` latches true and all slots go SUSPENDED (`sync.py:561`). For push providers `update_interval is None`, so there is no poll loop and `async_get_usercodes`/`_reset_backoff` never runs. Recovery depends solely on `push_update()` (`coordinator.py:123-125`). If the lock is reachable for reads but only fails writes (or the provider stops pushing), every slot stays SUSPENDED indefinitely with no poll to clear it — a silent stuck state. Consider an active connectivity re-probe on recovery, or not gating push-provider suspension on a write-only failure signal.

**MEDIUM — `note_connectivity_failure` records an unbounded consecutive count with no window**
`coordinator.py:129-137`. The lock breaker has no `window`, so `record_failure` never decays. Sync-path failures and poll-path failures both increment the same counter, and `backoff_delay` grows as `2**(count - threshold)`. A burst of sync write failures inflates `failure_count`, so the next genuine poll backoff jumps to `backoff_max` immediately. Not a silent failure, but the count no longer cleanly means "consecutive poll failures," which the warning log at `coordinator.py:147-152` still claims.

**LOW — slot breaker reset on every recovery cycle masks a non-converging code**
`sync.py:466-474` and `467` comment acknowledges this: a code that never converges restarts its attempt window on each connectivity recovery, so the slot-level breaker may never trip and the slot silently retries forever instead of suspending. Behavior is documented and intentional, but worth confirming it matches the spec.

The `CodeRejectedError`, `LockDisconnected/LockOperationFailed`, generic `Exception`, and post-sync `async_refresh` (`sync.py:659`) paths all log and set a defined next state correctly. No empty catches or swallowed errors.

Relevant files: `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/coordinator.py`, `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/sync.py`, `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/resilience.py`.
```

```
I have what I need. Let me trace the key behavioral gap precisely. The recovery-from-SUSPENDED logic in `_request_sync_check` (sync.py:466-474) gates resumption on `not self._coordinator.unreachable`. A slot-code suspension sets SUSPENDED while `unreachable` stays False. This means a slot-code-suspended slot would resume on the very first `_request_sync_check` (since `unreachable` is already False) — but the source comment claims recovery "restarts the attempt window each recovery cycle." Let me confirm there is no test covering recovery from a slot-code suspension.

The recovery test (`test_suspended_to_out_of_sync_on_coordinator_recovery`) explicitly trips and resets the *lock* breaker — that is purely the connectivity reason. No test sets SUSPENDED via the windowed slot breaker and then verifies resume behavior.

## Test Coverage Analysis: `refactor/resilience-circuit-breaker`

### Summary
Strong coverage overall. The `CircuitBreaker` primitive is well unit-tested across both policies; the coordinator's `unreachable` trip/clear is covered (`test_unreachable_reflects_backoff_trip`, `test_unreachable_clears_on_recovery`); slot isolation, connectivity coupling, and per-slot `slot_suspended` issue create+delete are all present. The five target behaviors are largely covered. There are two genuine behavioral gaps worth closing before merge.

### Critical Gaps

**1. Recovery-from-SUSPENDED is only tested for the connectivity reason, NOT the slot-code reason. (Criticality: 8)**
The asymmetry is real and risky. In `sync.py`, `_suspend_slot` (slot-code path, lines 418-442) sets `_state = SUSPENDED` but leaves `coordinator.unreachable == False`. The resume gate in `_request_sync_check` (lines 466-474) is `if not self._coordinator.unreachable`. So a slot-code-suspended slot satisfies the resume condition *immediately* on the very next state-change callback — there is no "wait for lock recovery" for that reason, despite the docstring claiming recovery "restarts the attempt window each recovery cycle."

`test_suspended_to_out_of_sync_on_coordinator_recovery` (test_sync.py:650) exercises only the connectivity path: it trips/resets the *lock* breaker. No test puts a slot into SUSPENDED via the windowed `_slot_breaker` and asserts the resume behavior. A future change to the resume gate (e.g., adding a per-slot suspend flag) could silently break or change resumption for non-converging codes with zero test failures. Add a test: drive `_suspend_slot`/windowed-breaker SUSPENDED, fire `_request_sync_check`, assert it resumes to OUT_OF_SYNC (and document that slot-code suspensions resume on the next check, not on lock recovery).

**2. No test that two config entries sharing a lock BOTH suspend on connectivity. (Criticality: 7)**
`test_two_entries_same_lock_share_suspension_and_recovery` (test_init.py:1060) trips the breaker and asserts only entry B reaches SUSPENDED; it never asserts entry A's manager also suspends, and entry A is left IN_SYNC (nothing to sync). The "shared lock breaker suspends all entries" contract — the whole point of lock-level coupling — is asserted for one entry only. A regression isolating the breaker per-entry would pass. Make both entries' slots out-of-sync, then assert both managers transition to SUSPENDED and both recover.

### Well Covered
Per-slot `slot_suspended` issue create (`test_suspend_creates_repair_issue`) and delete (`test_slot_suspended_issue_deleted_on_recovery`) including the slot-num-scoped issue ID; slot isolation in both layers; `test_set_disconnect_failures_trip_lock_breaker` correctly verifies set-side `LockDisconnected` feeds the lock breaker and the next tick suspends rather than hammering.
```

```
I now have full confirmation of the issues. Let me verify one more thing about the `_suspend_slot` resetting the breaker, which combined with the SUSPENDED resume creates a tight loop. I have all I need.

## Code Review: `refactor/resilience-circuit-breaker`

Reviewed `git diff main...HEAD` across `resilience.py` (new), `coordinator.py`, `sync.py`, `diagnostics.py`, `websocket.py`, strings/translations, and tests.

### Critical (90-100)

**1. Non-converging slot never stays suspended — tight retry loop (confidence: 92)**
`custom_components/lock_code_manager/sync.py:466-474` (and `:587-603`)

When a code "won't converge," `_suspend_slot` is entered while `coordinator.unreachable` is `False` (a slot trip does NOT trip the lock breaker — the new test at `tests/test_sync.py` asserts `failing._coordinator.unreachable is False`). But the SUSPENDED exit gate only checks `if not self._coordinator.unreachable:` and immediately resumes to `OUT_OF_SYNC`. Every coordinator update or tracked-entity state change fires `_request_sync_check` (`sync.py:696-697`), so a reachable lock resurrects the slot almost instantly. Worse, `_suspend_slot` calls `self._slot_breaker.reset()` (`sync.py:419`), so the freshly-resumed `OUT_OF_SYNC` slot starts from a zero count and must re-accumulate `MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS` (3) again before re-tripping.

Net effect: the "code won't converge" protection is defeated for a reachable lock — the slot oscillates SUSPENDED → OUT_OF_SYNC → 3 failed sets → SUSPENDED rather than latching. The old design latched lock-wide and only resumed on a real recovery signal. The intended "latches until `reset` is called" semantics described in `resilience.py:24-26` are not actually realized for the convergence case, because the only resume signal is the unrelated connectivity breaker. The SUSPENDED state needs its own latch (e.g. distinguish connectivity-suspend from convergence-suspend, and gate convergence resume on something that actually changed — like the sync target / PIN change via the IN_SYNC path, not on `unreachable`).

This is the core recovery-semantics question called out in the task, and the new test `test_non_converging_code_suspends_only_its_slot` only verifies the suspend transition, not that it stays suspended — so the regression is uncovered.

### Important (80-89)

**2. Convergence breaker can mis-trip across the recovery boundary (confidence: 80)**
`sync.py:374` + `sync.py:632`

On a `LockDisconnected`/`LockOperationFailed` during a set, `_perform_sync` has already called `self._slot_breaker.record_failure()` (`sync.py:374`) before the exception propagates, and the handler then also calls `coordinator.note_connectivity_failure()`. So a pure connectivity outage increments BOTH breakers. If the lock breaker recovers (poll succeeds) before the 5-minute slot window lapses, the slot breaker still carries connectivity-induced counts toward its convergence threshold, conflating "lock was offline" with "code won't converge." Consider only counting toward the slot breaker on a completed-but-unverified set, not on transport failures.

### Conventions
No violations found: no inline imports, no `nonlocal`/`global`, docstrings are prose, acronyms spelled out. `resilience.py` is clean and idiomatic.
```

Five commits: `CircuitBreaker` primitive → coordinator lock breaker → slot isolation → connectivity coupling → repair strings.

## Code review summary (4 specialized reviewers)

### 🔴 Critical — 1 (valid; it's a gap in *my* design reasoning)
**Slot-code suspension doesn't actually latch on a reachable lock** — `sync.py:466-474`.
The SUSPENDED→OUT_OF_SYNC resume gate only checks `not coordinator.unreachable`. A "code won't converge" suspend leaves `unreachable=False`, so *any* coordinator update or tracked-entity change resumes the slot almost immediately, `_suspend_slot` having reset the breaker — so it re-accumulates 3 attempts and re-trips, hot-looping.

I dismissed this during implementation as "matches today," but the reviewers are right and my reasoning was flawed: **today the suspend was lock-wide**, so no sibling kept the coordinator active. Now that it's isolated, sibling slots' activity (each sync triggers `async_refresh`) resumes the suspended slot every few seconds — which defeats the breaker's whole purpose. Fix direction: gate slot-code resume on the *sync target changing* (PIN/active change via the IN_SYNC path), not on `unreachable`.

### 🟠 Important — 1
**Push-only providers can stay SUSPENDED forever** — `coordinator.py` + `sync.py:623`.
Behavior Change 3 lets set-side write failures trip the lock breaker. Push providers have no poll loop (`update_interval=None`) and drift checks are skipped while tripped, so recovery depends entirely on an incoming `push_update`. If writes fail but the lock isn't pushing, `unreachable` never clears.

### 🟡 Suggestions
- **`CircuitBreaker` accepts incoherent configs** (both `window` and `backoff_*`, or neither) — type-analyzer rates invariant-expression 4/10. Cheap fix: a 3-line `__init__` guard asserting exactly one policy.
- **`note_connectivity_failure` inflates the no-window lock counter** with sync-path failures, so the "N consecutive poll failures" warning log is now slightly inaccurate.
- **Test gaps:** no test covers resume from a *slot-code* suspension (only connectivity); `test_two_entries_same_lock_share_suspension_and_recovery` only asserts one entry suspends.

### ✅ False positive (flagging so you can disregard)
code-reviewer's "important" #2 (transport failure increments both breakers) is wrong — `record_failure()` at `sync.py:374` runs *after* the awaited set, so it never executes on a raising set. Verified.

### Strengths noted
Clean idiomatic primitive, no convention violations, every except path logs + surfaces a repair issue, and the new unit tests make previously-untestable backoff/window logic directly testable.

---

My recommendation: the Critical finding is worth fixing before merge (it materially undermines the slot-code breaker), and it's cheap to address alongside the push-provider concern and the constructor guard. Want me to fix those, or are you taking it from here?

> AGENT

Done. Both review fixes are implemented, tested, committed, and pushed to **PR #1181**. Full suite: **911 passing**, all hooks green.

## What I changed (commit `f4ef9489`)

**🔴 Critical — slot-code suspension now latches**
A slot suspended for a non-converging code (or unexpected error) records its desired target `(active, pin)` at suspend time (`sync.py` `_suspend_slot`). The `_request_sync_check` resume gate now distinguishes the two reasons:
- **Code/exception suspend** → stays suspended until the desired target *changes* (user edits the PIN / toggles the slot) or the slot returns to sync — it no longer resumes on unrelated coordinator updates, so it can't hot-loop set attempts against a reachable lock.
- **Connectivity suspend** → still resumes as soon as the lock is reachable.

New test `test_code_suspension_latches_until_target_changes` proves it stays suspended on a same-target check and resumes on a target change (closing the recovery-coverage gap the test reviewer flagged).

**🟠 Important — probe-poll unreachable push locks**
When the lock breaker trips, the coordinator now sets `update_interval` to the backoff value for *all* providers (not just poll providers) and kicks an initial refresh via `note_connectivity_failure`, so a push lock whose writes fail with no incoming push starts polling to probe for recovery instead of staying suspended forever. On recovery, the original cadence (`None` for push) is restored, stopping the probe. The old `test_backoff_push_provider_does_not_change_interval` was inverted into `test_backoff_push_provider_polls_to_probe_recovery`, plus a new `test_note_connectivity_failure_kicks_probe_for_push`.

The MEDIUM log-accuracy finding is incidentally improved (warning now reads "polling every Ns until it recovers").

## Left as-is (out of the scope you set)
- **`CircuitBreaker` constructor guard** (type-analyzer's footgun: both/neither policy kwargs construct silently). A 3-line `__init__` assert would close it — happy to add if you want it.
- **Two-entries-both-suspend test gap** (#2): the shared-lock connectivity test still asserts only one entry. Minor coverage hardening.
- The local spec doc still describes the pre-fix recovery semantics — I can sync it if useful, but it's gitignored/local-only.

PR is ready for your review: **https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/pull/1181**

```
I have everything I need. Here is my analysis.

## Error/Failure Handling Review: `f4ef9489`

### (a) Fire-and-forget `async_create_task(async_request_refresh())` — coordinator.py:142
**Clean.** `async_request_refresh()` (base class, update_coordinator.py:305) routes through `_async_refresh`, whose final `except Exception` block (line 551-554) catches everything, sets `last_update_success = False`, and calls `self.logger.exception(...)`. So a probe failure cannot raise out of the orphaned task — it is logged at ERROR via the coordinator's logger, not silently swallowed. No unhandled-task warning, no silent drop. The transport failure also already flows through `_apply_backoff` independently. Good.

### (b) Probe-poll getting stuck or silently stopping — coordinator.py:144-194
**Clean, with one robustness note.** The probe loop is self-sustaining: each failed poll re-enters `_apply_backoff` (line 209) which re-asserts `update_interval = backoff_delay` (line 154-155), and `_reset_backoff` only restores `None` when `failure_count > 0` is cleared on an actual success (line 185-194). `update_interval` cannot be restored to `None` mid-outage because reset is gated on a successful fetch/push.

The one genuine dependency: the recovery probe for a push provider relies entirely on the single kicked refresh at line 142 establishing the polling cadence. That kick only fires on the `tripped and not was_tripped` transition. If that one task is dropped (e.g. `hass` shutting down) the cadence is still set by `_apply_backoff` synchronously before the task is scheduled (line 140 runs first, 154-155 sets the interval) — so the interval is armed regardless of whether the kicked task runs. Not stuck. Fine.

### (c) Latch path with `_resolve_slot_state()` returning None — sync.py:488-497
**Clean — not a silent failure.** If resolution returns None while code-suspended, `_request_sync_check` leaves the slot SUSPENDED. That is correct: a None resolution means entities/coordinator data are not yet readable (sync.py:296-315), so there is no basis to resume, and the persistent fixable repair issue (sync.py:443) remains visible to the user. Staying suspended is the safe, surfaced state, not a swallowed error. Resumption is also reachable via the target-changed branch on the next check once state resolves.

### Verdict
No genuine silent-failure or stuck-state defects in this commit. Relevant files: `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/coordinator.py` and `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/sync.py`.
```

```
## Test Coverage Analysis: commit f4ef9489

**Summary.** Both fixes have targeted new tests that exercise the changed lines. The push-probe poll-and-recover path is well covered; the code-suspension latch has two genuine behavioral gaps tied directly to branches the fix introduced.

**Critical Gaps**

**(a) Code-suspended slot resuming via the `calculate_in_sync` branch — MISSING. Criticality 8.**
`_request_sync_check` (sync.py:489-492) resumes a code-suspended slot under two OR'd conditions: target changed, OR `calculate_in_sync(slot_state)` is true. `test_code_suspension_latches_until_target_changes` only proves the *target-changed* leg (it mutates `_code_suspend_target` to a different tuple). The `calculate_in_sync` leg — the slot drifts back into sync on its own (e.g. lock now reports the expected code) while the target is unchanged — has zero coverage. If a future refactor drops that `or self.calculate_in_sync(...)` clause, a slot that genuinely reconverged would stay permanently suspended and no test would fail. This is the realistic stuck-forever bug.

**(c) Code vs. connectivity suspension not conflated — PARTIALLY MISSING. Criticality 7.**
The existing `test_suspended_to_out_of_sync_on_coordinator_recovery` resumes on reachability, but it sets `_state = SUSPENDED` directly, leaving `_code_suspend_target = None`, so it only proves the connectivity leg in isolation. No test asserts the discriminator: a *code-suspended* slot (target set) must NOT resume merely because the lock is reachable, while a *connectivity-suspended* slot (target None) must. The latch test confirms reachability alone doesn't resume, but never contrasts it against a connectivity suspension clearing on the same reachable lock — so a regression collapsing the two branches could pass.

**Well Covered**

**(b) Push-probe recovery — FULLY ASSERTED.** `test_backoff_push_provider_polls_to_probe_recovery` asserts the successful poll both clears the breaker (`unreachable is False`) and restores `update_interval` to `None`. The set-side kick test correctly asserts the trip-transition `async_request_refresh` fires exactly once. Good behavioral coverage, resilient to refactoring.

**Recommendation:** add one test for the `calculate_in_sync` resume leg and one contrasting code-vs-connectivity suspension on a reachable lock. Both are short additions to `tests/test_sync.py` mirroring the existing latch test.

Relevant files: `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/sync.py` (lines 481-503), `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/test_sync.py` (lines 650-686, 885-918).
```

```
## Review: commit `f4ef9489` on `refactor/resilience-circuit-breaker`

Reviewed `coordinator.py` (`note_connectivity_failure`, `_apply_backoff`, `_reset_backoff`), `sync.py` (`_suspend_slot`, `_request_sync_check`, `_async_tick_impl`), and the `CircuitBreaker` semantics in `resilience.py`.

Both fixes look correct. No high-confidence, important issues found.

**(a) Latch logic — sound.** The only exit from `SUSPENDED` is `_request_sync_check` (sync.py:481); the tick is a no-op for `SUSPENDED` (sync.py:530), so a code-suspended slot can never be silently re-suspended with `target=None` while the lock is unreachable, nor reach `IN_SYNC` bypassing the clear. A reachable lock that keeps rejecting an unchanged `(active, pin)` stays latched — the intended behavior, not a stuck-state bug. It resumes correctly on target change or `calculate_in_sync` (sync.py:489-496). No legitimate stuck-forever scenario.

**(b) Probe-poll — sound.** `async_create_task(async_request_refresh())` from a sync `@callback` is the correct HA pattern (it's debounced, so it can't pile up). The `was_tripped` / `not was_tripped` guard (coordinator.py:139-142) fires the task only on the trip *transition*, so repeated failures don't re-schedule. Setting `update_interval` mid-flight is safe — `DataUpdateCoordinator` picks it up at the next `_schedule_refresh`, not immediately, so no double-schedule or leaked timer. The unconditional restore (coordinator.py:194) has no edge case here: the lock breaker has **no window** (coordinator.py:63-67), so it only untrips via explicit `reset()` inside `_reset_backoff`, which restores `update_interval` in the same block. There's no window-expiry path that could leave the backoff interval stuck.

**(c) `_code_suspend_target` clearing — correct.** Set only in `_suspend_slot` (sync.py:435); cleared on the single `SUSPENDED → OUT_OF_SYNC` transition (sync.py:495). Since that transition is the *only* way out of `SUSPENDED`, a later connectivity-only suspension always starts from `target=None` and is classified correctly.

One minor note (below the report threshold, ~55 confidence): the `# type: ignore[assignment]` at coordinator.py:194 is now needed because `_original_update_interval` may be `None`; fine as-is.

The fixes are correct.
```
