I have everything I need. Both files read fully, shared helpers confirmed, test coverage enumerated.

## Feasibility Assessment: Shared base for `schlage.py` / `akuvox.py`

### 1. Genuinely identical vs structurally-similar-but-divergent

**Already shared (no work needed):** `_parse_tag`/`_make_tagged_name`/`parse_slot_num` (`_util.py:19-49`), `is_masked_or_empty`, `managed_slots`, `async_call_service`, `SlotCode` classification primitives — all in `_base.py`.

**Genuinely identical (per-provider, duplicated):**
- `async_setup` — both just `super().async_setup()` + `_async_tag_unmanaged_*()` (schlage:126-129, akuvox:158-161).
- `async_hard_refresh_codes` — identical two-liner (schlage:390-398, akuvox:163-166).
- Properties `supports_code_slot_events` (both False), `usercode_scan_interval` (only the timedelta value differs: 5min vs 2min).
- The *shape* of `async_clear_usercode`: list/get → find entry whose `_parse_tag` slot == code_slot → delete → return bool (schlage:359-388, akuvox:319-344). Differs only in the lookup key (name vs device id) and an extra `_is_local_user` filter on akuvox.

**Structurally similar but materially divergent:**
- `async_get_usercodes`: schlage returns `UNREADABLE_CODE`/`EMPTY` only (write-only PINs, schlage:288-292); akuvox returns the **real PIN** (akuvox:260-261). Schlage adds dedup-by-code_id + out-of-range logging (schlage:265-286); akuvox filters cloud users instead.
- `_async_tag_unmanaged_*`: schlage **clear-before-add via add+delete rename, with rollback** on delete failure (schlage:194-227) and a masked-PIN skip (167-173); akuvox **renames in place via `modify_user`**, no rollback, no masked check (akuvox:212-223). Slot-allocation bookkeeping also differs (index-walk vs `sorted(managed - assigned)`).
- `async_set_usercode`: schlage **always clears then adds** + swallows "already exists" for cloud eventual-consistency (schlage:328-350); akuvox **modify-in-place if exists else add** (akuvox:305-310), no consistency handling.
- Service layer: different domains, service names, `service_data` vs `target` argument shapes, dict-vs-list responses (`get_codes`→dict, `list_users`→list).
- akuvox-only: `_is_local_user`, schedule/relay defaults (akuvox:34-50).

### 2. Recommendation: a thin mixin only; reject a template-method base

A full template-method base (abstract `_get_entries`/`_add`/`_delete`/`_modify` + shared orchestration) is a **net complexity increase**. The bodies that would become "shared" carry the real divergence — rollback vs none, clear-before-add vs modify-in-place, readable vs masked, "already exists" handling. Forcing them into one template means each abstract seam sprouts a flag or hook (`supports_modify`, `rolls_back`, `pin_is_readable`, response-normalizer). That trades ~40 honestly-duplicated lines for conditional indirection across two divergent backends — the classic two-instances-isn't-a-pattern trap. Akuvox's modify-in-place vs schlage's delete-then-add are genuinely different algorithms, not parameterizations.

**Worth doing — a small `_TaggedNameLock` mixin** holding only the truly-identical, parameterless pieces:
- `async_setup` (calls abstract `_async_tag_unmanaged()`), `async_hard_refresh_codes`, `supports_code_slot_events=False`.
- A `_find_entry_for_slot(entries, slot)` helper returning the matching entry (each provider supplies its iterable + an `_is_relevant` predicate that akuvox overrides for `_is_local_user`).

Realistic savings: **~25-35 lines**, modest readability gain, low risk. Keep `async_get_usercodes`, `async_set_usercode`, `_async_tag_unmanaged_*` per-provider. Do **not** abstract the service layer.

### 3. Protecting tests
Strong, symmetric coverage exists. `tests/providers/schlage/test_provider.py` (25 tests incl. rollback-on-delete-failure :262, already-exists-as-success :376, duplicate-tag :162) and `tests/providers/akuvox/test_provider.py` (23 tests incl. failed-modify-doesnt-consume-slot :477, skips-cloud-users :199). Both have `test_e2e.py` (7/7) exercising set/clear/get through full coordinator setup. This suite would catch regressions in the divergent paths, so even the conservative mixin is safe to attempt. The divergent methods being well-tested is precisely the signal that they should *stay separate*.

## 1. `providers/matter.py` internal complexity

**Repeated error-translation boilerplate (highest value).** Six call sites (`async_setup` L154-163, `_set_lock_credential` L375-393, `_clear_lock_credential` L404-419, `async_get_usercodes` L435-444, `async_clear_usercode` L592-608) wrap a Matter helper in the identical `try/except ServiceValidationError -> LockCodeManagerProviderError; except HomeAssistantError -> LockDisconnected` pattern, differing only in the helper name string. Extract a single helper, e.g. `await self._call("get_lock_users", get_lock_users, client, node)` that formats both messages from one name arg. **~35 lines saved. Risk: needs-care** (must preserve `SetCredentialFailedError` re-raise in `_set_lock_credential`, which is the one exception to the pattern — keep its `except SetCredentialFailedError: raise` first).

> AGENT

This is the key finding, and it confirms your instinct:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Providers currently raise `LockDisconnected` for *operation* failures, not just connectivity.** `zha.py:292`/`:317` and `zigbee2mqtt.py:527`/`:574` literally do `raise LockDisconnected(f"Failed to set/clear PIN: {err}")`, and the shared `async_call_service` wrapper (`_base.py:854`) re-raises *any* service-call failure as `LockDisconnected`. So a reachable lock that simply *rejects/fails an operation* is currently mislabeled as "disconnected." Post-#1181 that's not cosmetic: `LockDisconnected` during a set feeds the **lock-level circuit breaker** (`note_connectivity_failure`), nudging the whole lock toward "unreachable/suspended" — when really only one operation failed. `LockOperationFailed` is exactly the missing "the lock is up but the operation failed, just retry" signal.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

So **yes** — your read is right. The proper wiring is a focused two-part behavior change (separate from the safe pass): (1) raise `LockOperationFailed` instead of `LockDisconnected` at the operation-failed sites (zha/zigbee2mqtt set/clear failures; the generic `async_call_service` failure path); (2) in `sync.py`, have `LockOperationFailed` retry **without** feeding the connectivity breaker (only `LockDisconnected` should). I'll come back to that with a concrete proposal after the safe pass.

=== diff stat ===
 .../lock_code_manager/binary_sensor.py             | 10 ++-----

=== new util helper ===
def get_slot_coordinator(
    config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry,
    lock: BaseLock,
    slot_num: int,
) -> LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator | None:
    """
    Return the lock's coordinator, or None if it is not yet available.

    When the coordinator is missing a warning is logged so the slot
    entities are simply skipped rather than raising during setup.
    """
    coordinator = lock.coordinator
    if coordinator is None:
        _LOGGER.warning(
            "%s (%s): Coordinator missing for lock %s when adding slot %s entities",
            config_entry.entry_id,
            config_entry.title,
            lock.lock.entity_id,
            slot_num,
        )
    return coordinator

> AGENT

Now, back to your `LockOperationFailed` question — here's the concrete proposal (a focused, separate behavior change, since it's outside the "safe" pass):

## Proposed wiring for `LockOperationFailed`

**Part 1 — raise it where an operation fails on a *reachable* lock.** The unambiguous sites are where the command was sent and the operation itself failed (today mislabeled `LockDisconnected`):
- `zha.py:292` (`Failed to set PIN`) → `LockOperationFailed`
- `zha.py:317` (`Failed to clear PIN`) → `LockOperationFailed`
- `zigbee2mqtt.py:527` (`Failed to set PIN`) → `LockOperationFailed`
- `zigbee2mqtt.py:574` (`Failed to clear PIN`) → `LockOperationFailed`

Genuine connectivity checks ("Lock not connected", "MQTT not available", device-unavailable) **stay** `LockDisconnected`. I'd leave matter as-is — it already has nuanced handling (`CodeRejectedError`/`DuplicateCodeError` for rejections), and its `HomeAssistantError`→`LockDisconnected` mapping is genuinely ambiguous.

**Part 2 — make `sync.py` treat the two differently** (currently they share one `except` and both feed the connectivity breaker):
- `LockOperationFailed` → retry next tick, **do not** call `note_connectivity_failure()`.
- `LockDisconnected` → retry **and** feed the lock breaker (unchanged).

That's the payoff: a reachable lock that fails one operation just retries, instead of being nudged toward "unreachable" and suspending all its slots.

**Test impact:** the zha/zigbee2mqtt provider tests that assert set/clear-failure raises `LockDisconnected` would flip to `LockOperationFailed`, plus a new sync test asserting `LockOperationFailed` doesn't trip the lock breaker. `test_sync.py::TestLockOperationFailedRetry` already covers the retry path.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This is a genuine bug the consolidation surfaced, not just tidiness: before #1181, `LockDisconnected`-vs-`LockOperationFailed` were equivalent (both retried), so mislabeling was harmless. #1181 gave `LockDisconnected` a *side effect* (feeding the lock-level breaker), which retroactively made the mislabeling matter — a single rejected write on a healthy lock can now cascade into lock-wide suspension. Wiring `LockOperationFailed` correctly closes that gap.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Want me to do this as a follow-up PR (behavior change, ~4 provider sites + the sync split + test updates)?

> DEVELOPER

yes, but I wonder, should operation failed not call the lock breaker eventually? if something is broken for a long time we don't want to retry for a long time

> AGENT

Yes — it should eventually break. And thinking it through, the right breaker is the **slot** breaker, not the lock breaker — for a concrete reason:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The lock-level breaker recovers by **probe-polling** (a *read*). But an operation failure is a failing *write* on a lock whose reads may be perfectly fine ("writes broken, reads OK"). If `LockOperationFailed` fed the lock breaker, the recovery probe (a read) would succeed → clear the breaker → resume → the write fails again → loop. The lock breaker's recovery signal doesn't validate the path that's actually broken, so it's the wrong tool. The slot breaker recovers on *target change / return-to-sync*, not a read-probe — so it won't spuriously un-suspend a slot whose writes keep failing.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

There's also a nice unification: the slot breaker already counts "set attempts that didn't achieve sync" (the completed-but-didn't-converge case). Feeding raised `LockOperationFailed` into it too means the breaker means one clean thing — **"3 failed sync attempts in 5 min → suspend this slot"** — whether the attempt raised an error or silently didn't take. No double-counting (a set either completes → records in `_perform_sync`, or raises → records in the `except`).

## Final design
- **Part 1** — `zha`/`zigbee2mqtt` set/clear *operation-failed* sites raise `LockOperationFailed` (connectivity checks stay `LockDisconnected`).
- **Part 2** (`sync.py`, split the shared `except`):
  - `except LockDisconnected` → `note_connectivity_failure()` + retry (unchanged: lock-wide, auto-recovers on read-probe — correct for connectivity).
  - `except LockOperationFailed` → `self._slot_breaker.record_failure()` + retry. Repeated failures accumulate → tick trips the slot breaker → suspends just that slot (with a repair issue). **Does not** touch the connectivity breaker.

So a transient op failure retries a few times; a persistent one suspends the slot after 3-in-5min and stops hammering — isolated to the affected slot, exactly your concern. Tests: flip the zha/zigbee2mqtt failure assertions to `LockOperationFailed`, and add a sync test that repeated `LockOperationFailed` trips the slot breaker (not the lock breaker).

Good to implement it this way?

> AGENT

I'll drop the `STATE_ON` gate too — and it's safe because the latch-recovery already detects active-state changes: if a set-suspended slot is later disabled, `_request_sync_check` sees `(OFF, pin) != (ON, pin)`, resets, and lets the clear proceed. So both set *and* clear operation failures can circuit-break via the slot breaker without stranding a re-disabled slot.

{

  "old_string": "        except (LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed) as err:\n            _LOGGER.info(\n                \"%s: Lock operation failed during %s usercode: %s. Will retry on next tick.\",\n                self._log_prefix,\n                \"set\" if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON else \"clear\",\n                err,\n            )\n            # Feed the lock breaker so repeated connectivity failures during\n            # sync converge to \"unreachable\" alongside poll failures.\n            self._coordinator.note_connectivity_failure()\n            self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n            return",
  "new_string": "        except LockDisconnected as err:\n            _LOGGER.info(\n                \"%s: Lock unreachable during %s usercode: %s. Will retry on next tick.\",\n                self._log_prefix,\n                \"set\" if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON else \"clear\",\n                err,\n            )\n            # Connectivity failure: feed the lock breaker so repeated failures\n            # converge to \"unreachable\" alongside poll failures (recovers via a\n            # successful poll/push).\n            self._coordinator.note_connectivity_failure()\n            self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n            return\n        except LockOperationFailed as err:\n            _LOGGER.info(\n                \"%s: Operation failed during %s usercode: %s. Will retry on next tick.\",\n                self._log_prefix,\n                \"set\" if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON else \"clear\",\n                err,\n            )\n            # The lock is reachable but the operation failed. Count toward the\n            # slot breaker so a persistently-failing slot suspends instead of\n            # retrying forever -- NOT the lock breaker, whose read-probe\n            # recovery can't validate a failing write.\n            self._slot_breaker.record_failure()\n            self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n            return"
}

Since #1181, a set-side `LockDisconnected` feeds the lock-level connectivity breaker — so a single failed write could push the whole lock toward \"unreachable\" and suspend *every* slot, even though only one operation failed.\n\n**The fix:**\n- `zha` and `zigbee2mqtt` raise `LockOperationFailed` (not `LockDisconnected`) when the set/clear operation itself fails. Genuine connectivity checks (\"Lock not connected\", \"MQTT not available\", etc.) stay `LockDisconnected`.\n- `sync.py` splits the handler:\n  - `LockDisconnected` → feeds the lock breaker (recovers via a successful poll/push — correct for connectivity).\n  - `LockOperationFailed` → feeds the **slot** breaker, so a persistently-failing slot suspends after the 3-in-5-min window instead of retrying forever — and does **not** mark the lock unreachable. (The lock breaker's recovery is a read-probe, which can't validate a failing *write*, so it's the wrong tool here.)\n- The slot-breaker trip is no longer gated on the slot being active, so a failing *clear* can also break; a re-disabled slot still recovers via the existing target-change detection in `_request_sync_check`.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [x] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [ ] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- Tests: flipped the zha/zigbee2mqtt set/clear-failure assertions to `LockOperationFailed`; added a sync test that repeated `LockOperationFailed` trips the slot breaker (suspends the slot) while leaving `coordinator.unreachable` False. Full suite: 910 passing.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -2",

> AGENT

Done — shipped as **PR #1184**: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/pull/1184 (910 tests passing, hooks green).

## What it does
- **zha / zigbee2mqtt**: a failed set/clear *operation* now raises `LockOperationFailed` instead of `LockDisconnected`; genuine connectivity checks stay `LockDisconnected`.
- **sync.py**: the shared handler is split —
  - `LockDisconnected` → lock breaker (auto-recovers via poll/push), unchanged.
  - `LockOperationFailed` → **slot** breaker → suspends just that slot after 3-in-5-min instead of retrying forever, and never marks the lock unreachable.
- Dropped the active-slot gate on the slot-breaker trip so failing **clears** can break too; re-disabled slots still recover via the existing target-change detection.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This closes the loop you spotted: pre-#1181, `LockDisconnected` vs `LockOperationFailed` were interchangeable (both just retried), so the mislabeling was harmless. #1181 gave `LockDisconnected` a *side effect* (feeding the connectivity breaker), which retroactively turned a cosmetic naming sloppiness into a real cascade risk — one rejected write suspending a whole lock. The right breaker turned out to be decided by the **recovery mechanism**: the lock breaker recovers via a read-probe, which can't prove a *write* is healthy again, so operation failures had to go to the slot breaker.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Open PRs (independent, disjoint files — merge in any order)
- **#1183** — second-pass complexity trims (matter inline, dead `EntityNotFoundError`, coordinator-guard helper).
- **#1184** — this `LockOperationFailed` fix.

Both are off current `main`. Want me to watch their CI, or are we good here?

> AGENT

Investigated — and **completing the migration is not the clean change it looks like**, which actually validates scoping #1184 the way I did:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The remaining write failures flow through the shared `BaseLock.async_call_service`, which wraps `(HomeAssistantError, OSError)` as `LockDisconnected`. The tempting fix — split it (`OSError`→`LockDisconnected`, `HomeAssistantError`→`LockOperationFailed`) — would break callers, because **schlage and akuvox internally `except LockDisconnected`** from that wrapper for their tagging/rollback logic (`schlage.py:196,208,219,336`; `akuvox.py:214`). Changing the wrapper's exception type silently defeats those handlers (e.g. schlage's delete-rollback at :219). That's exactly the divergent, well-tested per-provider logic the earlier review said to leave alone. So the shared-wrapper path can't be migrated without reworking each provider's nuanced error handling.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

My assessment of Copilot's two examples:
- **`async_call_service` (zwave/schlage/akuvox writes)** — real gap, but blocked: a blanket split breaks schlage/akuvox's `except LockDisconnected` handlers. zwave alone *could* migrate (it doesn't catch it), but doing only zwave would make the contract even more uneven.
- **zwave post-write verify** — this is a *read* (`get_usercode_from_node` to confirm the write landed); a failure there is the node not responding, so `LockDisconnected` (→ recovers via poll) is genuinely defensible, not a mislabel.

**Net:** #1184 is a strict improvement with no regression — the other providers behave exactly as before (write failure → lock breaker → auto-recovers via probe). It correctly migrated the two providers that have a clean, separate write-failure site. Fully completing the contract means reworking schlage/akuvox's entangled rollback/tagging error handling — a bigger, riskier change against the providers we explicitly chose to keep separate.

My recommendation: **merge #1184 as-is**, and I'll reply to the Copilot thread documenting the scope + why the shared-wrapper path is deferred. If you want, I can file a follow-up issue for "migrate `async_call_service` write failures to `LockOperationFailed` (requires reworking schlage/akuvox `except LockDisconnected` handlers)."

Want me to (a) post that reply on the PR thread and leave #1184 as-is, or (b) take a swing at the fuller migration now?

    async def _async_on_integration_loaded(self) -> None:
        """
        Handle provider integration LOADED transition.

        Re-runs ``async_setup`` to re-initialize provider state (e.g.
        re-register event listeners after an integration reload), then
        refreshes the coordinator and subscribes to push updates.

        Operations are chained sequentially so setup completes before
        the coordinator refresh or push subscription begins.
        """
        if (
            self._lcm_config_entry is None
            or self._setup_running
            or not self.lock_config_entry
            or self.lock_config_entry.state != ConfigEntryState.LOADED
        ):
            return

        self._setup_running = True
        try:
            await self.async_setup(self._lcm_config_entry)
        except LockDisconnected:
            LOGGER.debug(

All checks pass. Here is the report.

## Report

### Goal
Migrate write-operation failures to raise `LockOperationFailed` (lock reachable, operation rejected) instead of `LockDisconnected` (connectivity), consistently across providers.

### Source changes

**`custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/_base.py`**
- L47: added `LockOperationFailed` to the exceptions import.
- L843-866 `async_call_service`: split `except (HomeAssistantError, OSError)` into two handlers. `OSError` -> `LockDisconnected` (transient connectivity); `HomeAssistantError` -> `LockOperationFailed` (ServiceValidationError + HA-wrapped failures). Preserved the existing log line in both. `CancelledError`/`TypeError`/`KeyError` still propagate.
- L463 `async_setup_internal`: `except LockDisconnected as err` -> `except (LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed) as err`.
- L502 `_async_on_integration_loaded`: `except LockDisconnected` -> `except (LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed)`.
- Left the `setup_push_subscription()` `except LockDisconnected` (L399) untouched as instructed.

**`custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/schlage.py`**
- Import: added `LockOperationFailed`.
- `_async_tag_unmanaged_codes`: tag-add (L197), delete-original (L209), rollback-delete (L220) catches -> `(LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed)`.
- `async_set_usercode`: `contextlib.suppress(LockDisconnected)` -> `suppress(LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed)`; the "already exists" handler (now catching `LockOperationFailed`, `str(err).lower()` still works).

**`custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/akuvox.py`**
- Import: added `LockOperationFailed`.
- `_async_tag_unmanaged_users`: `except LockDisconnected` -> `except (LockDisconnected, LockOperationFailed)` around `_async_modify_user`.
- Did not touch zha/zigbee2mqtt (they don't use `async_call_service`).

### Test changes (all: split by error type, not weakened)
- `tests/providers/test_base.py`: the HA-error `async_call_service` test now expects `LockOperationFailed` (renamed `..._raises_operation_failed_on_error`); imported `LockOperationFailed`. The OSError test stays `LockDisconnected` (unchanged).
- `tests/providers/schlage/test_provider.py`: 5 assertions that simulate `HomeAssistantError`/`ServiceValidationError` now expect `LockOperationFailed` (set/clear/get failures + "non-exists still raises"). The "already exists" and rollback tests needed no assertion change (they check behavior/call-counts, not the type) and still pass.
- `tests/providers/akuvox/test_provider.py`: 4 assertions simulating `HomeAssistantError` now expect `LockOperationFailed` (set/clear/list failures + invalid-response, which raises via HA's service layer).
- `tests/providers/zwave_js/`: not touched — its `LockDisconnected` assertions are verification-poll/`FailedZWaveCommand` paths that don't go through `async_call_service`; they remain `LockDisconnected` and pass.

### Results
- `uv run pytest tests/ -q`: **910 passed**.
- `prek run --all-files`: **all hooks pass** (ruff, format, mypy, flake8, pydocstyle, etc.).
- Not committed, per instructions.

### Flag (judgment call worth noting)
ruff 0.15.12 reformatted the two `except (A, B):` clauses that have no `as` binding (`_base.py` `_async_on_integration_loaded` and `akuvox.py` `_async_tag_unmanaged_users`) into the parenthesis-less form `except A, B:`. This is ruff's canonical output under the project's Python 3.14 target (PEP 758 makes parentheses optional when there is no `as`). It compiles, lints, type-checks, and the runtime behavior is verified by `test_failed_modify_does_not_consume_slot` (raises `HomeAssistantError` -> `LockOperationFailed` -> caught). The `as`-bound clauses keep their parentheses. I deferred to the project formatter rather than fighting it; if you prefer the explicit-parens style everywhere, that would require a ruff config change.

> AGENT

Confirmed: `requires-python = ">=3.14"`, ruff `target-version = "py314"`. The core split is correct (`OSError`→`LockDisconnected`, `HomeAssistantError`→`LockOperationFailed`), and the full suite is **910 green**.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This retroactively corrects something I told you earlier. The `except (X, Y):` clauses that kept showing up as `except X, Y:` were **not** a "display/rendering artifact" — that was my misdiagnosis. The project targets Python 3.14 (`requires-python = ">=3.14"`, ruff `py314`), so **PEP 758** is in effect: parentheses are *optional* in `except` clauses that have no `as` binding, and ruff actively rewrites them to the parens-less form. So `except TypeError, ValueError:` was genuinely on disk, genuinely valid, and ruff-canonical — which is also exactly why Copilot keeps flagging it (it doesn't assume a 3.14 target). The code was always correct; my explanation of *why* was wrong.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

Done — the full migration is pushed to **#1184** (`14d70ce3`), which resolves Copilot's substantive comment.

## What landed
- **`async_call_service` now splits by error type:** `OSError` (timeout/ConnectionError) → `LockDisconnected` (connectivity → lock breaker, auto-recovers via poll); `HomeAssistantError`/`ServiceValidationError` → `LockOperationFailed` (operation rejected → slot breaker). This covers the **zwave, schlage, and akuvox** write paths that flow through the shared wrapper.
- **Updated the handlers that wrap those writes** to catch both types (they mean "the write didn't succeed"): schlage tag-add / delete-original / rollback / `suppress` / "already exists", akuvox tag, and the base setup-retry handlers. zwave's post-write *verify* (a read) stays `LockDisconnected` — correct, as discussed.
- **Tests split by error type** (not weakened): the shared `ServiceProviderConnectionTests` mixin, schlage, and akuvox now expect `LockOperationFailed` for `HomeAssistantError`-driven failures and keep `LockDisconnected` for `OSError`; schlage's rollback/eventual-consistency tests pass unchanged.

**910 tests pass, all hooks green.** Now every provider is consistent: a reachable lock that fails a write suspends only that slot instead of cascading to a lock-wide "unreachable."

Two notes:
- **Correction owed:** the parens-less `except X, Y:` I earlier blamed on a "display artifact" is actually **PEP 758** (valid on the project's Python-3.14 target, ruff-enforced). Same reason Copilot keeps flagging it — it doesn't assume 3.14. The code was always fine; my explanation wasn't.
- This is why Copilot's `_util.py` comment on the *other* PRs was a false positive, and its `async_call_service` comment here was a true positive — worth a quick reply on the thread if you'd like.
